own windows: the toolbar button, the shortcut, the options page, `chrome://extensions/shortcuts`, the worker's
console), `recipes/s9-redirect.py` (port 8083: every GET → 302 to the same path on 8081; 8082 is held by a
Windows process, S4 ledger). Fixtures: `python -m http.server` on 8080 **and** 8081, both `--directory
test-fixtures` (the 8081 copy is the blocked origin); test patterns are fixture or reserved names only:
`localhost:8081`, `bank.example`, and the invalid line `not a host!`.*

*Windows and tabs (all the driver's own; the user's windows and tabs are never touched, named or captured):
**W** — `chrome.exe --new-window http://localhost:8080/e2e-playground.html?s9=w`, found by HWND diff; its tab
(`<T#w>`, retitled `S9 W human tab` by `s9.ts setup`) is the human's tab while W is the last-focused normal window;
**O** — the options page, opened by typing `chrome-extension://<id>/options.html` into the omnibox of a fresh
`about:blank` window; **E** — `chrome://extensions/shortcuts`, then `chrome://extensions` (the worker's DevTools
**D** opens from its card); the SDK's own background tabs `a`, `c` (8081), `b5`, `t4` (`s9.ts setup`, in W).
`<id>` is `cpnemjbgecfbeicldpaaegbhjgjgjfep` (§1 item 9). Actor **M** is one persistent MCP actor, started before
SAFE-1 and ended right after its SAFE-5 leg (its lease on `<T#c>` must end before SAFE-5d); a fresh actor **M2**
serves SAFE-9 and is ended at the teardown; each cursor is claimed by its first untargeted tool (Step 8 S5).
(*Amended 2026-10-24, prep fixer r1: was "ended at the teardown", S4-prep-verifier finding 2.*)*

*Pass rules, written now: a row passes when every `CHECK` line it names says `PASS` and every quoted text matches
character for character (the live verifier compares the record with this table row by row, S4.3); "record, not
a check" rows pass by being recorded; "not measured" and "unit-tested only" rows are listed so the record is
complete and never count as a pass. **An `ok` is not evidence**: every row reads a refusal's code and exact
message, a page reporter (the counter button's name `Click counter: N`, the `#trusted-input` length, a
`visibilitychange` log), a UIA read, or a transcript line. A failed row is recorded, never fixed in the wave. The
safety state is restored to the state found before the next row, except where rows chain (said in the row).*

**The found state and the restore (S4.4).** No safety key has ever been written in this profile (the extension in
Chrome runs a pre-Step-9 build, S4 ledger), and `parseStoredSafety` (`extension/src/safety-state.ts`) reads absent
keys as the defaults `{armed: true, evaluate: true, blockList: ""}` (#171). So the found state is expected to read
`safety armed=true evaluate=true blockedCount=0` (`daemon.status`) and, on O, `armed=true evaluate=true count=0
lines=0 sha256=e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855` (the sha256 of the empty text)
with the status line `Bridge armed.`. ~~Whatever is found is what SAFE-11 restores; a non-empty block list is saved
by `Read-S9SafetyState -SaveTo` to `…\scratchpad\s4-found\` outside the repo (never printed, never cat'd) and
deleted after the read-back matches.~~ *Superseded 2026-10-24 (S4 prep fixer r1, before any row ran): was "whatever
is found is what SAFE-11 restores", with a non-empty found list saved to the scratchpad, because every toggle in
the wave is a blind press and the restore targets the default (S4-prep-verifier finding 4).* **Guard:** a found
state other than armed + JavaScript on + an empty list (on O, read from a fresh page, or in `daemon.status`) stops
the wave before any toggle (`Test-S9FoundState`); nothing is changed and the user's list is never copied. After the
wave the three keys exist with the found values: absent and
present-with-default read identically (`parseStoredSafety`); the keys cannot be removed (the writers test refuses
`storage.local.remove`, #188), so the restore is judged on the read values and the hash (S4.4), not on key
presence. **Every read-back is of the stored state** (`Read-S9StoredState`: O is sent to `about:blank` and to a
fresh options page, which fills its controls from `storage.local`; the read waits until `#safety-status` is
rendered), cross-checked with `daemon.status` (`s9.ts wait-safety`). **On any stop or helper throw** from the
driver's first window on, the wave runs `Restore-S9All` (`recipes/s9-win.ps1`: the safety restore and its
read-back, then the pin state, the keyboard layout and the driver windows); a restore that does not read back
equal is a STOP that escalates (G9-1) — the wave sheet's stop rule.

*Note 2026-10-24 (after Run 13): SAFE-9's last clause expects the daemon's `safety state: …` line after the reload's
`extension resync` line; the daemon writes it one line before, by design — `daemon/src/intercept.ts:278` logs the safety
line when the hello's `safetyState` arrives, while `:243` logs `extension resync` only after its `listTabs` returns (Run
13: both at 14:31:26Z, the safety line first; SAFE-0's at 14:13:39Z the same way). The row is not edited after its run:
that clause stays **FAIL (expected text)** for Run 13; a later wave's copy of the row should expect it before ([`S4-live-verifier.md`](plans/2026-10-24-step9-phase-e-safety-steps/S4-live-verifier.md) finding 4).*

*Note 2026-10-24 (after Run 13): SAFE-10 (console set-up) expects the worker console window titled with the extension's
own URL; Chrome 156 titles the service worker's DevTools window just `DevTools` (8 characters). That the window is the
worker's console is shown by its content (SAFE-10's console half, 27 `safety: disarmed by the user` hits). The row is not
edited after its run: the set-up row stays **FAIL (expected text)** for Run 13 ([`S4-live-verifier.md`](plans/2026-10-24-step9-phase-e-safety-steps/S4-live-verifier.md) finding 3).*

### 4.21 R14 rows (Step 10 S1, Run 14: Step 9's live follow-ups)

*Corrected 2026-10-27: R14-5c expects the 8081 target logged once, "since the hop is still requested"; as built,
Step 11b's rule stops the hop, so a re-run on the Step 11b build expects 0 hits (Run 17 RD-3; Step 11b phase doc § As
built). The row stays as Run 14 ran it.*

*Added 2026-10-25 by Step 10 stage S1's prep builder, before any R14 row ran, on the build already on disk (Step 9's
code; daemon build `99bb55c5…`; no `npm run build` in this wave — phase doc 0.1). These rows are **re-measurements as new
rows**: §4.20's rows and Run 13's results are never edited (decisions.md · 2026-10-24 · S4: Dated notes on §4.20 for
two expected-text errors (#195)). Sources: phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-24-step10-step8-follow-ups.md` 0.10 (quoted
under each row), decisions.md · 2026-10-24 · S4: SAFE-5c's redirect half, SAFE-POP-1, SAFE-3 and SAFE-4(b) are
re-measured in the next live wave (Step 10) (#194), · S4: SAFE-REL-3 prints a URL class, not "withheld" (#196), · S4:
The redirect server uses port 8083 (#197), · S4: Live runners return on every stop; one stop rule for sheet and brief
(#193); Stage 0 F20 and F22 (`…-steps/stage0-audit.md`). Every expected text below is printed by the committed helpers
and tied to the product's constants by `recipes/test/s9-rules.test.ts` (the drift tests of §4.20 plus the R14 ones:
`BLOCKED_URL_MESSAGE`, the fixture's `Open popup` link, `describeDebuggerError`'s `DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS`, the extension's
wrapper schemes, the options page's `Bridge armed.`); the event and log ORDER was re-read in the code (#195's lesson:
`daemon/src/intercept.ts` logs `safety state: …` when the extension's `safetyState` arrives, before `extension resync`,
which waits for its `listTabs`).*

*How the wave runs: [`S1-wave-sheet.md`](plans/2026-10-24-step10-step8-follow-ups-steps/S1-wave-sheet.md) — every block
is one call of `recipes/s9-r14.ps1 -Block <name>` (the wave's globals kept between calls; one stop procedure). Helpers
(written before the run; their rules tested by `npm test`, typechecked by `npx tsc --noEmit -p recipes/tsconfig.s9.json`):
`recipes/s9.ts` (`ROW …`/`CHECK …` lines as in §4.20; Run 14 adds `navigate-class`, `download-file`, `pop14`, `r14-type`,
`redirect-probe`, `server-logs`, `rect-check`, `wait-safety --guard`; every throw is one `STOP: …` line, exit 2),
`recipes/s9-rules.ts` (the pure rules), `recipes/s9-win.ps1` (UI Automation in the driver's own windows; Run 14 adds the
window placement, the 20 s banner poll, UIA-only pinning, `STOP:` lines, the raw output file, a proven fresh options page
for every stored read — S4-live-verifier finding 7), `recipes/s9-redirect.py` (now a `ThreadingHTTPServer` on 8083),
`recipes/s9-r14.ps1` (the block runner). Fixtures: `python -m http.server` on 8080 and 8081 (`--directory
test-fixtures`, 127.0.0.1); the only test pattern is `localhost:8081`. Windows (the driver's own; the user's windows and
tabs are never touched, named or captured): **W** (`…?s9=w`, its tab retitled `S9 W human tab`), **E**
(`chrome://extensions`), **D** (the worker's DevTools, titled `DevTools` on Chrome 156 — printed `<DevTools window>`),
**O** (the options page); the SDK's background tabs `a`, `c` (8081), `b5`, `t4` (`s9.ts setup`), `r3`. No headless MCP
actor runs in this wave.*

*Pass rules, written now (as §4.20): a row passes when every `CHECK` line it names says `PASS` and every quoted text
matches character for character (`<…>` = a value recorded as observed; `…` after a toolbar name = Chrome 156's second
line, compared on the first line only); "RECORD" clauses pass by being recorded and are never judged; `NOT MEASURED`
never counts as a pass. **An `ok` is not evidence**: each row reads a refusal's code and message, the page's own
reporter (`#trusted-input`'s value), a server log count, or a UIA read. A failed row is recorded, never fixed in the
wave. **The toolbar button:** R14-3 (the re-arm click) and R14-4b (the disarm and the re-arm) need the pinned button;
no row uses `Alt+Shift+K` (R14-3's text names "the toolbar click", and in R14-4b the keys would land in a window whose
page is receiving trusted typing); every other change goes through the options page. **Before any state change** the
pin state is read by UIA (block `window`): pinned → go on; not pinned → one UIA-only attempt (no mouse); still not
pinned → `STOP: … HUMAN GATE (pinning) …` and the runner returns (#192/#202 do not pre-approve a mouse click).*

*Amended 2026-10-25 by the S1 prep fixer (round 1), still before any R14 row ran, applying the step agent's rulings on
`…-steps/S1-prep-verifier.md` F1–F8, N1–N5 (report `…-steps/S1-prep-fixer1.md`): SAFE-0's tuple is read before any
reload (F3); R14-REL-3's `not listed` / `hidden=disarmed` are NOT MEASURED (F4); R14-3's guard needs no occluder (F5);
R14-4b has its own type verdict (F6); R14-LOGS judges the console by S1.6 as written (F2); the restore prints the
block-list hash comparison, never a non-empty hash (F7/F8); the teardown closes r3 explicitly (N1); the background
`r14-type` is tracked and ended before the restore on any stop (F1). The block-list COUNT may be printed (the daemon
logs it by design, #132); the sha256 of a non-empty list never is; the empty text's sha256 may be.*

**STOP RULE (#193): on any STOP line — a restore that fails, a guard that prints STOP, a human gate (pinning, a
grant), any throw — run the restore first if the safety state was touched since the found-state record, record its
read-back, write the ledger, and RETURN to the step agent. A helper may be fixed only between rows, before any
observation of the next row, and never past a STOP line.**

names changed; its expected text and pass rule did not. `-Block r3` now calls `Find-S9FreePlacement` where it called

them (`s9.ts placement-order`); each try moves the driver windows E and D inside W's rect (off O's rect), places W and
O side by side, and prints `ROW R14-3 placement-attempt-<n> …` and `PLACEMENT attempt <n> on work area […]: free|not
free — …` (records, no CHECK); the first free try is kept (`PLACEMENT chosen: attempt <n> of <m>`), none free → the
same `STOP: … R14-3 placement guard …`. The guard (`placement-before-switch`, `placement-before-click`) is unchanged;
its ROW line now ends with ` · occluder ids driver:<W|E|D|O>, class=<ClassName> process=<name>, …` when the count is
not 0 (never a title). Attempt 3 does not re-run R14-REL-3 or R14-POP-1 (recorded in attempt 2), and R14-LOGS counts
`daemon.log` from attempt 2's wave mark (the step agent's rulings (a), (b), (d) on Return 2). Round 2 (ruling on
`…-steps/S1-wave-verifier.md` F1): each try's area is the work area with its bottom cut to the top of any shell taskbar
window (`Shell_TrayWnd`, `Shell_SecondaryTrayWnd`) whose rect intersects it, an auto-hidden one's visible strip
included, printed as `PLACEMENT work area […] cut to y=<n> by taskbar class=<class>`; a fixed rule with no margin.
The guard is unchanged: any window above O that overlaps it, a taskbar included, still counts and still STOPs.*

*Note 2026-10-25 (S1 wave fixer round 3, after Run 14 attempt 3's Return 3; `…-steps/S1-wave-fixer.md` § Round 3):
procedure steps changed, expected texts and pass rules did not. R14-3 is recorded NOT MEASURED in Run 14 (attempt 3
observed its disarm half, then stopped at `placement-before-click` on a non-driver Chrome window) and is not re-run;
attempt 4 runs R14-4b, R14-5c-ctl, R14-5c, R14-RESTORE, R14-LOGS and R14-TEARDOWN. Before each guard a transient Chrome
window could trip — the toolbar click's foreground and `FromPoint` checks (R14-4b's press and re-arm), the omnibox
foreground/focus check, the one-new-window check, the layout tap and the window count — the driver parks the mouse
cursor at a fixed point, 4 px right of and below the top-left corner of W's page area, and again after each
`FromPoint`-verified click (`PARK cursor at (x,y) in W's page area (…)`, a record). No guard is loosened. The placement
guard lines also print, when there is an occluder, `OCCLUDER <i> …` (rect, owner and root owner, styles) and
`CURSOR at (x,y)` (diagnostics, never a title).*

### 4.22 R15 rows (Step 10 S7, Run 15: Step 10's own rows)

*Added 2026-10-25 by Step 10 stage S7's prep builder, before any R15 row ran and before the Step 10 build was loaded
(phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-24-step10-step8-follow-ups.md` "Stage S7", exit checks S7.1–S7.6). These rows measure
Step 10's code: 0.2 (MCP trusted tools focus first, #106, #214), 0.3 (the 800-cap warning, #117, #206, #216), 0.4/0.5
(`saveTo`, #127, #205, #207, #217, #218), 0.8 (SW-1, #209 item 3), 0.9 (bb-login's wait, #215), 0.12 (`JS_DISABLED`,
#202, #219), and Run 14's two follow-ups (R15-POP-2 #211, R15-3 #212). RET-1 (0.7, G10-2) and the pp1 leg (0.5, G10-3)
are **prepared here and NOT run**: the record says `not run — waits for the user` (S7.5). §4.20 and §4.21 are not
edited (their rows and results stand; re-measurements are new rows).*

*Superseded 2026-10-25: was "RET-1 (0.7, G10-2) and the pp1 leg (0.5, G10-3) are prepared here and NOT run: the record
says `not run — waits for the user`", because the user granted both (decisions.md · 2026-10-25 · The user grants
RET-1's Chrome kill (G10-2) and the pp1 leg (G10-3) once for Run 15; the button is already pinned (#221), widened by
#222) and both ran in Run 15 after the teardown: PP1 PASS, RET-1 NOT MEASURED (the relaunch helper failed after the
kill), RET-1R PASS (§5 Run 15). The heading "Prepared, NOT run" over the RET-1/RET-1R/PP1 table below is read with
this note; the rows' text is unchanged.*

*How the wave runs: [`S7-wave-sheet.md`](plans/2026-10-24-step10-step8-follow-ups-steps/S7-wave-sheet.md) — every block
is one call of `recipes/s10-r15.ps1 -Block <name>` (Run 14's model, #213: one stop procedure, a narrow resume). Helpers
(written before the run; rules tested by `npm test` — `recipes/test/s10-rules.test.ts`, `s10-fixtures.test.ts` —
typechecked by `npx tsc --noEmit -p recipes/tsconfig.s10.json`): `recipes/s10.ts` (the SDK side; `ROW …`/`CHECK …`
lines; every throw one `STOP: …` line, exit 2), `recipes/s10-rules.ts` (the pure rules and the expected texts, each tied
to its product constant or source line by the drift tests), `recipes/s10-win.ps1` (dot-sources `s9-win.ps1` unchanged;
adds the R15-3 placement with the row as a parameter, the bb-login window close, the memory sampler),
`recipes/s10-mem.ps1`, `recipes/s10-r15.ps1`; reused unchanged: `recipes/s9.ts` (`safe0`, `call`, `wait-safety`,
`wait-alone`, `probe`, `cleanup`, `daemon-shutdown`, `rect-check`, `placement-order`, `park-point`), `s9-actor.ts` (the
headless MCP actors), `s6.ts rep0` (REP-0), `recipes/bb-login.ps1`. Fixtures: `test-fixtures/s10-mcpf.html` (a page
reporter of every click / input / keydown with `isTrusted`), `s10-cap.html` (exactly 1 000 listable buttons `Cap item
0001…1000`), `s10-save.html` (deterministic values and the page's own `TextEncoder` + `crypto.subtle` sha256), served by
`python -m http.server 8080 --directory test-fixtures` on 127.0.0.1 (also as `saveblk.localhost:8080`, which Chrome
resolves to loopback); `recipes/fixtures/` on **8085** for BBL-1. Windows (the driver's own; the user's windows and
tabs are never touched, named or captured): **W** (`…/e2e-playground.html?s9=w`, retitled `S9 W human tab`), **E**
(`chrome://extensions`), **D** (the worker's DevTools, `<DevTools window>`), **O** (the options page), **R** (REP-0's
own, `?t5=s6-human`, closed after it), bb-login's own windows (closed by handle after each leg). The SDK's background
tabs in W (`s10.ts setup`, opened after the reload): `a` (`…?s9=a`), `mcpf`, `sw` (`s10-mcpf.html`), `cap`, `save`,
`close`, `blk` (`s10-save.html`; `blk` on `saveblk.localhost:8080`), `pop`, `jsd` (`e2e-playground.html`). Headless
MCP actors: `claude -p --model sonnet --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config <only chrome-agent-bridge> --allowedTools
mcp__chrome-agent-bridge` (`recipes/s9-actor.ts`), each prompt built by `s10-rules.ts PROMPTS` and sent with an argument
array (no shell), each naming its `tab` explicitly — an explicit `tab` neither claims the tab nor moves the MCP cursor,
and the setup client's leases end with it, so the actors' tabs are unheld; each actor is ended before the next
client needs its tab (`s9.ts wait-alone`).*

*Pass rules, written now: a row passes when every `CHECK` line it names says `PASS` and every quoted text matches
character for character (`<…>` = a value recorded as observed). "RECORD" clauses pass by being recorded and are never
judged; `NOT MEASURED` never counts as a pass. **An `ok` is never evidence**: each row reads the page's own reporter
(`window.__s10log`, `window.__s10cap`, the field's value), the page's own sha256, the file on disk, a refusal's code /
message / data, a server log count, or a UIA read. A failed row is recorded and the wave goes on; it is never re-run
or re-tuned. **Never printed or committed:** the token; the block-list pattern (`saveblk.localhost` is counted, never
printed by a helper line); a `saveTo` path (printed `<saveTo>`; the files are `%TEMP%\bridge-e2e\s15\sv\s15v-*.txt`,
deleted at teardown); an expression (`<expr>`); any value (hashes, sizes, counts only); a URL or title of the user's
tabs; a screenshot.*

**STOP RULE (#193): on any STOP line — a restore that fails, a guard that prints STOP, a human gate (pinning, a
grant), any throw — run the restore first if the safety state was touched since the found-state record, record its
read-back, write the ledger, and RETURN to the step agent. A helper may be fixed only between rows, before any
observation of the next row, and never past a STOP line.**

*Amended 2026-10-25 by the S7 prep fixer (round 1), before any R15 row ran: every row block builds the page values it
reads at its start, so a resume that skipped an earlier block still measures (C1, M3; `blockDependencyGaps` in
`recipes/test/s10-rules.test.ts`); MCPF-1 runs as three blocks and SAVE-FIX, SAVE-64's MCP leg and SAVE-PRIM as their
own, each block bounded by a budget so that it ends under the tool's 600 s cap (m2; the sheet §1 lists each); the
tracked-file guards ignore only the steps dir (M1); SW-1 checks its tab before each capture (M2); a scan that cannot
find the token is NOT MEASURED (NIT). No pass rule's threshold or verdict text changed: SW-1 gains a per-trial
pre-condition, SAFE-0 a known limit, and the new lines the fixes print are added to the expected text where they
appear; the block numbers below follow the new order.*

A failed capture makes its d not measured and the outcome undetermined. The table counts only measured trials and needs 3/3 measured for "runs to its bound" or "the event ends the wait", else "undetermined" (`swPre`, `swMeasured`, tested with a not-measured trial). | `s10.ts` |

### 4.23 R16 rows (Step 11 S5, Run 16: the upload rows, SAVE-REFUSE-R and SW-2)

*Corrected 2026-10-27: the K-opt rows (K-opt-stand, K-opt-home and their `options-model.ts` lines) are Run 16's texts;
as built, S0F-a refuses a home folder (`UPLOAD_HOME_REASON`, no `UPLOAD_HOME_WARNING`) and the lines moved: the
current texts are §4.24's K17 (Step 11b phase doc § As built S0F-a).*

*Added 2026-10-25 by Step 11 slice S5-sheet (Claude Sonnet 5.5), before any Run 16 row ran and before the Step 11 build was loaded (exit checks S5.1, R.2). Sources: phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-25-step11-file-upload.md` 0.6, 0.12 with its correction (#251), 0.14 (a) and (c), § Stage S5, § Exit checks S5.1–S5.7, § Human gates; decisions.md #243, #245, #246, #251, #254, #262, #266, #267, #268, #271; `docs/plans/2026-10-25-step11-file-upload-steps/S3.md` §7 (twelve items; table at the end). §4.20–§4.22 are not edited; SAVE-REFUSE's and SW-1R's Run 15 records stand. Commands are placeholders (`⟨helper: …⟩`): S5-helpers and S5-driver build them to match this section, never the other way round. Fixtures are `s11-*` pages made by S5-fixtures; the CSP page is served on port 8084 (#251).*

**Pass rules, written before any observation.** A row passes when every clause named in its "Pass rule" holds and every quoted text matches character for character (`<…>` = a value recorded as observed). `RECORD` clauses pass by being recorded; `NOT MEASURED` never counts as a pass; **an `ok` is never evidence.** Every upload row is judged by the page's own reporter (count, names, sizes and SHA-256 of what the page received, in order, and `isTrusted` of each `change` event; its SHA-256 equals the sha256 recorded when the wave made the file) and by a MutationObserver record of `data-__bridge-upload` (added, then removed; carriers left = 0 in the light DOM, every open shadow root and every same-origin frame). **Every refusal row:** the reporter holds 0 files, saw no `change` and no `input` event, and the mark was never set. A failed row is recorded and the wave goes on; it is never re-run, re-tuned or fixed in the wave (a fix is a proposed decision). Never printed or committed: the token; the text of the block list or the Upload folders box (counts, and the sha256 only inside the restore comparison); a path under the user's profile (printed `<ROOT>\name`, `<OUTSIDE>\name`); the sentinel folder's name; the block alias; an expression or a `saveTo` path; a title or URL of a non-fixture tab; a screenshot. Uploads go only to fixture pages on 127.0.0.1 / `*.localhost` (G11-2).

**STOP rule (#193).** On any STOP line (a failed restore, a guard printing STOP, a human gate such as an unpinned toolbar button, a port held by a program the wave did not start (#251), an unreadable file-URL toggle, a found state that is not armed with JavaScript on, a tracked file changed outside the steps dir, any throw after the first state change) the runner restores first if the safety state was touched, records the read-back, writes the ledger and returns. A helper may be fixed only between rows, never past a STOP line.

**Slices and order (#271).** Each row's "Live" column says where it runs. **liveA** (no file access needed): reload and loaded-build proof (UP-L1), found state (UP-0), UP-OPT, UP-FA (only when the toggle reads OFF), UP-6, UP-7, SAVE-REFUSE-R, SW-2, then UP-RESTORE and UP-LOGS. **liveB** (upload rows, only with file access on: found ON, or G11-3 granted): UP-0 again (liveB's own found record), ROOT added as UP-OPT does (no new row), UP-1…UP-5, UP-6B, UP-8, UP-9, UP-PATCH, UP-TWICE, UP-RAW, UP-NAV, UP-DEVT, UP-STALE, UP-7J, UP-RACE-A, UP-RACE-B, then UP-RESTORE and UP-LOGS again. Each slice leaves the safety state as found, read back by count and hash (S5.4). Within liveA the order is the row order of the table below where it is liveA.

**G11-3 branch (#245).** UP-0 reads the extension's "Allow access to file URLs" toggle on its details page, read only, in a window the wave opened; the wave never presses or changes it. **ON:** the liveB rows run; UP-FA records `not run — the toggle is on`. **OFF:** UP-FA runs (RECORD); each of these 17 rows is recorded `NOT RUN — waits for G11-3` and liveB does not start: UP-1, UP-2, UP-3, UP-4, UP-5, UP-6B, UP-8, UP-9, UP-PATCH, UP-TWICE, UP-RAW, UP-NAV, UP-DEVT, UP-STALE, UP-7J, UP-RACE-A, UP-RACE-B. UP-9 is on this list only because the phase doc (0.12, as corrected by #251) lists it; its refusal is in fact decided by the gate, before the file-access check, and this sheet does not override the phase doc. These **still run** (their decisive answer comes before the handler's file-access check, or needs no upload): UP-L1, UP-0, UP-OPT, UP-FA, UP-6, UP-7, SAVE-REFUSE-R, SW-2, UP-RESTORE, UP-LOGS. **Unreadable** toggle (no or conflicting stateful element): STOP, nothing changed. If a liveB row answers the file-access refusal while UP-0 read ON, that row is FAIL "UP-0's reading is contradicted"; it is not re-read. The found state is never changed by the wave except as #243 allows (the Upload folders box and the block list, restored exactly).

**Windows and state changes.** Only windows the wave opened (the human's tab is the wave's own `W` tab). State changes go through the options page in the wave's own window or the toolbar button (pinned; an unpinned button is a STOP, never a mouse click). The wave records what it finds (armed, JavaScript, block list and Upload folders as counts and sha256, the texts kept in files outside the repo) and restores exactly that; the guard is armed with JavaScript on, any block list and any Upload folders text. Each changing row restores its own change before the next row.

| K-map | `BAD_PARAMS`, no `data` | The value is a Map, which JSON turns into {} — not saveable; convert it first (e.g. Object.fromEntries(map)) | `save.ts:116` |
| K-cycle | `PAGE_ERROR`, `data {"name":"TypeError"}` | JSON.stringify threw on the value in the page (a cycle, a BigInt, or a throwing toJSON or getter) — nothing was saved | `save.ts:121-122` `STRINGIFY_FAILED_MESSAGE` (Run 15's SAVE-REFUSE row, §4.22, records the same `data`) |

Never allow your home folder, or any folder that holds bridge.token or a .ssh folder. An agent that can also run programs on this computer can put any file into an allowed folder. | `extension/src/options-model.ts:100-101` `UPLOAD_FOLDERS_WARNING` |
| K-opt-root | invalid-line reason | a whole drive or the filesystem root is never allowed | `options-model.ts:103` `UPLOAD_ROOT_REASON` |
| K-opt-rel | invalid-line reason | not an absolute folder path: write it from the drive letter (C:\…) or from / (/home/…) | `options-model.ts:105` `UPLOAD_RELATIVE_REASON` |
| K-opt-home | warning | a home folder, or a folder that holds home folders: every file in it (keys, tokens, documents) could be uploaded | `options-model.ts:109` `UPLOAD_HOME_WARNING` |

*Known limits, recorded before the run (not failures):* the file-URL toggle's UI Automation exposure on this Chrome, F12 docking DevTools in window X, the banner's visibility after a detach, and `createIsolatedWorld` without `Page.enable` are unmeasured until this wave; a helper that cannot read what it needs prints a `STOP:` or records NOT MEASURED, and a fix between rows is a recorded set-up fix, never a change of a pass rule. The Run 16 record goes to `docs/plans/2026-10-25-step11-file-upload-steps/S5-run-record.md` and §5 gets a Run 16 section from S5-record.

cases and UP-LOGS's fixture set, ruled before Run 16 (#277)):* **SW-2** — a warm-up trial that is itself not measured,
or a trial count other than one warm-up plus three, makes SW-2 NOT MEASURED (never a decided outcome). **UP-LOGS** — a
URL counts as fixture only when it is `http://` on the wave's own fixture servers (127.0.0.1, `localhost` and its `*.localhost`
aliases on ports 8081 and 8084; bare `localhost` added the same day, before any observation, #278), `chrome://extensions`, or this extension's own `chrome-extension://<id>/` pages;
any other `scheme://host`, another loopback port included, counts as other.

### 4.24 R17 rows (Step 11b S4-sheet, Run 17: redirected requests, Step 11's follow-ups, Run 16's carried rows)

*Written 2026-10-27 by Step 11b slice S4-sheet (Claude Sonnet 5.5), before any Run 17 row ran and before the Step 11b build was loaded (exit checks D4.1, D4.2). It replaces the placeholder rows S0F-b, S3a and S3b left here (ids merged or kept, mapping at the end). Sources: phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-26-step11b-block-list-redirects.md` 0.3–0.7, 0.10, 0.11, the Stages note, § Exit checks D4.1–D4.6, § Human gates, § STOP rules; decisions.md #288, #290, #292, #293, #295, #296, #298–#303; `…-steps/stage0-audit.md` §3, F9, F11, F17; the Run 17 notes of S0F-a, S0F-b, S3a, S3b. §4.20–§4.23 and their records are not edited (Run 16's rows keep their ids; the carried rows below have new ones). Every expected text is a committed constant, re-read from the code on 2026-10-27 (table K17; rows quote it by key, never by paraphrase), so `recipes/test` drift tests of the S4-helpers can compare each one by constant name.*

**Pass rules, written before any observation.** A row passes when every clause of its "Pass rule" holds and every quoted text matches character for character (`<…>` = a value recorded as observed). `RECORD` clauses pass by being recorded; `NOT MEASURED` never counts as a pass; **an `ok` is never evidence**: the evidence is a refusal's code and message, a server-log count by query tag, a rule count, the page's own reporter, or a UIA read. **Hits are counted per query tag** (`s11b=<tag>`, one tag per call; every server's log line holds the request path with its query), never by total, so a favicon or a retry cannot count. A failed row is recorded and the wave goes on; it is never re-run, re-tuned or fixed in the wave (a fix is a proposed decision); timing bounds below were written now, not after seeing a time. Never printed or committed: the token, the text of the block list or the Upload folders box (counts; a hash only inside a restore comparison, or the empty text's `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855`), a rule or a filter (counts only, as Chrome's rule text may quote the list), a path under the user's profile (printed `<ROOT>\name`, `<OUTSIDE>\name`), a title or URL of a non-fixture tab, a screenshot. Fixtures only on 127.0.0.1 / `localhost` (G11b-2).

**STOP rule (#193).** On any STOP line (a failed restore, a guard printing STOP, a human gate such as an unpinned toolbar button, a port held by a program the wave did not start, a found state that is not armed with JavaScript on or whose block list matches a fixture URL, a tracked file changed outside the steps dir, any throw after the first state change) the runner restores first if the safety state was touched, records the read-back, writes the ledger and returns. **Row-level STOPs** (phase doc § STOP rules): a covered call that proceeds after its rules failed to install; a rule left installed after its call ended (`RULES` > 0 after a reply, or RD-4's stuck-rule hit); a bridge call answering differently from its pre-Step-11b text on a page with no redirect; a pattern, URL, rule or token in a log; the human's browsing changed (RD-6 reads 0); proof that Chrome rendered an own page or a `file:` page after a redirect (RD-7: an extension context in the tab, or the options page's own content read by UIA; RD-8: a `file:` document rendered). A refused or cancelled hop that leaves the tab reading the own URL is not that proof (Stage 0: the hop is cancelled, an error page commits under the own URL, so K-own is the safe answer). A helper may be fixed only between rows, never past a STOP line.

**Fixtures and ports.** `netstat -ano` on 2026-10-26 20:17 UTC (this slice; filtered for local ports 8080–8099 and 9234: no line, exit 1; 278 lines in all): 8080–8099 and 9234 free, daemon not running. 8080 is not used (VS Code may hold it, #273). The preflight re-checks every port below and stops on a holder the wave did not start; port 8090 must stay **unbound** (it is the closed port).

| Port | Server (127.0.0.1 only, started by the wave, stderr/stdout to a log outside the repo) | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| 8081 | `python -m http.server 8081 --bind 127.0.0.1 --directory test-fixtures` (allowed pages: `e2e-playground.html`, `s11-*`, `dl/hello.txt`) | every setup tab, history pages, RD-REBUILD-NARROW's tab |
| 8083 | `recipes/s9-redirect.py 8083 http://localhost:8085` (threaded, 302 at once; #197) | RD-1 … RD-6, RD-DL-* |
| 8085 | the **blocked fixture**: `python -m http.server 8085 --bind 127.0.0.1 --directory test-fixtures`, per-request log kept | the block list's one test pattern `localhost:8085` (**BLK**); its tagged hit count is the evidence |
| 8086 | `s11b-delay (.py)` (new, in lane; `recipes/s9-redirect.py` is not changed): sleeps 4 s, then 302 → `http://localhost:8085` | RD-5b, RD-DISARM |
| 8087 | slow allowed server: answers `/slow/…` after 3 s (page `e2e-playground.html`, file `hello.txt`) (new, `s11b-slow (.py)`) | RD-REBUILD-NARROW, RD-DL-SOCKET |
| 8088 | `s9-redirect.py 8088 chrome-extension://<EXTID>` | RD-7 (request `/options.html?…`) |
| 8089 | `s9-redirect.py 8089 file://` | RD-8 (request path = the wave-made file's `file:` path) |
| 8090 | nothing listens | RD-DL-ERR |
| 8091 | `s11b-flip (.py)` (new, in lane): the first GET of a path+query answers 200 (a small page, `Cache-Control: no-store`), every later GET of it 302 → `http://localhost:8085` | RD-BACK, RD-FWD |

**Slices and order (#271).** **liveA** (no file access needed) then **liveB** (only with "Allow access to file URLs" ON, read by RD-0 as §4.23's UP-0, never pressed; OFF → F-RAW, F-RAW-2, F-RAW-DIR, F-RAW-ENC, F-RAW-OK, R17-UP-RACE-A, R17-UP-DEVT are written `NOT RUN — waits for G11-3` and liveB does not start; an unreadable toggle is a STOP). Prologue (ledger, not rows): preflight, servers, files (`<ROOT>`, `<OUTSIDE>`, a folder inside ROOT, the `ü` file), window, **console** (the worker DevTools window opened and its capture started **before RD-0** and kept to RD-LOGS), reload by `recipes/reload-extension.ps1` (#65, one re-run on exit 2, #99). Order of liveA: **RD-0 (first line of the record), R17-UP-L1** (before any state change), RD-CTL, F-FILETAB, then the options-only typing rows **F-HOME, RD-CAPS-WARN, RD-REGEX**; then the helper builds RD-BACK's and RD-FWD's tab histories with the list as found (RD-FWD's tab is also moved back twice there); then **group G1** (list = found + BLK, saved once, read back by count and hash): RD-1, RD-6, RD-2, RD-3, RD-DL-ERR, RD-DL-HOP, RD-7, RD-8, RD-BACK, RD-FWD, RD-BACK-SAMEDOC, RD-REBUILD-NARROW, RD-DISARM; list restored to found (read back); then the single-change rows **RD-4, RD-5, RD-5b, RD-BACK-EMPTY, RD-DL-SOCKET**; then **R17-UP-OPT** (the first Upload-folders change), R17-UP-6, R17-UP-7, R17-SAVE-REFUSE; then **RD-RESTORE** and **RD-LOGS** (liveA). liveB: RD-0's found record again, ROOT added as R17-UP-OPT does, F-RAW, F-RAW-2, F-RAW-DIR, F-RAW-ENC, F-RAW-OK, R17-UP-RACE-A, R17-UP-DEVT, then RD-RESTORE and RD-LOGS again. A group or row that changes the safety state restores it before the next group (read back by count and hash). Only windows the wave opened are touched; the toolbar button must be pinned (RD-DISARM, R17-UP-7, R17-UP-RACE-A; an unpinned button is a STOP, never a mouse click).

**Helper abbreviations.** `R17 <block>` = `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\recipes\s11b-r17.ps1 -Block <block>` (new; the block runner, cloned from `s11-r16.ps1`, one block per row id in lower case, `liveA`/`liveB` run a slice). `B17 <sub>` = `node --import tsx s11b (.ts) <sub>` (new SDK side). `S9 …` = `node --import tsx recipes/s9.ts …`, `S11 …` = `… recipes/s11.ts …` (exist: `S9 call <ROW> <step> <op> … --expect`, ops `navigate`, `newtab`, `snapshot`, `tabs`, `status`, `download-file`, `close`; `S9 redirect-probe`, `wait-safety`, `scan-log`, `scan-file`; `S11 foundcheck|found|l1|up6|up7|raw|race|devt|saverefuse-sdk|saverefuse-mcp|restore-check|scan`). Line forms the new helpers print (the sheet's pass rules read them): `MANIFEST dnrWithHostAccess=<b> staticRuleSets=<absent|present>`, `RULES <n>` and `RULES samples=[…] min=<a> max=<b>`, `HITS <tag> redirect=<n> blocked=<n>`, `LAT <row> <ms> ms`, `REGEX <label> supported=<b> len=<n>`.

**Expected texts (table K17: the single source of every row; a row's text is the constant, no prefix, no suffix).** Re-read at HEAD `6d6490a`.

| Key | Code | Exact text | Source (constant) |
|---|---|---|---|
| K-url | `DOMAIN_BLOCKED`, no `data` | That address is on the user's block list in Chrome; the bridge will not open or fetch it. Ask the user. | `extension/src/safety.ts` `BLOCKED_URL_MESSAGE` (also `handlers/download.ts` for a blocked hop; R14-5c's text) |
| K-tab | `DOMAIN_BLOCKED`, no `data` | This tab shows a site on the user's block list in Chrome; the bridge will not read or act on it. Ask the user. | `safety.ts` `BLOCKED_TAB_MESSAGE` |
| K-own | `DOMAIN_BLOCKED`, no `data` | This tab shows one of the bridge extension's own pages, which the bridge never reads or acts on. | `safety.ts` `OWN_PAGE_MESSAGE` |
| K-filepage | `DOMAIN_BLOCKED`, no `data` | This tab shows a local file (a file: page), which the bridge never reads or acts on. | `safety.ts` `FILE_PAGE_MESSAGE` |
| K-fileurl | `DOMAIN_BLOCKED`, no `data` | The bridge never opens local files (file: URLs). | `safety.ts` `FILE_URL_MESSAGE` |
| K-disarmed | `DISARMED`, `data {"scope":"all"}` | The user has turned the bridge off in Chrome, so every page call is refused. Stop and ask the user to turn it back on; do not retry. | `safety.ts` `DISARMED_ALL_MESSAGE` |
| K-gate | `UPLOAD_REFUSED`, no `data` | That file is not in a folder the user allows for uploads in Chrome (extension options → Upload folders); the bridge will not upload it. Ask the user. | `safety.ts` `UPLOAD_REFUSED_MESSAGE` |
| K-notarget | `BAD_PARAMS` | uploadFiles needs a ref or a locator naming the file input | `extension/src/handlers/upload.ts` `UPLOAD_TARGET_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` |
| K-missing / K-relative | `BAD_PARAMS` (SDK preflight, no call) | `upload: paths[0] does not exist` / `upload: paths[0] is not an absolute path — pass an absolute file path` | `sdk/src/upload.ts` (the `upload: ` lead is `refuse`'s) |
| K-raw-missing | `BAD_PARAMS`, no `data` | upload: paths[<i>] does not exist (or Chrome cannot read it); nothing was uploaded | `extension/src/upload-exists.ts` `uploadProbeRefusal` (outcome `missing`) |
| K-raw-dir | `BAD_PARAMS`, no `data` | upload: paths[<i>] is a folder, not a file — pass a file; nothing was uploaded | `uploadProbeRefusal` (outcome `folder`) |
| K-devt | `DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS`, no `data` | Chrome DevTools (or another extension) is already attached to this tab — Chrome allows one debugger per tab. Close DevTools on that tab and retry. | `extension/src/debugger-errors.ts` |
| K-undef / K-node / K-map | `BAD_PARAMS`, no `data` | The value is undefined — nothing to save / The value is a DOM node, which is not saveable — return its text or HTML (e.g. el.outerHTML) / The value is a Map, which JSON turns into {} — not saveable; convert it first (e.g. Object.fromEntries(map)) | `extension/src/evaluate/save.ts` `REFUSALS` (`undefined`, `node`, `map`) |
| K-cycle | `PAGE_ERROR`, `data {"name":"TypeError"}` | JSON.stringify threw on the value in the page (a cycle, a BigInt, or a throwing toJSON or getter) — nothing was saved | `save.ts` `STRINGIFY_FAILED_MESSAGE` |
| K-opt-stand | options page | An agent may upload any file inside these folders to any site it can open. Allow only folders that hold files you mean to share. A home folder, or a folder that holds home folders, is refused; never allow a folder that holds bridge.token or a .ssh folder. An agent that can also run programs on this computer can put any file into an allowed folder. | `extension/src/options-model.ts` `UPLOAD_FOLDERS_WARNING` (changed by S0F-a; Run 16's text stays its record) |
| K-opt-home | invalid-line reason | a home folder, or a folder that holds home folders, is never allowed: every file in it (keys, tokens, documents) could be uploaded. Allow a folder inside it instead | `options-model.ts` `UPLOAD_HOME_REASON` |
| K-opt-root / K-opt-rel | invalid-line reason | a whole drive or the filesystem root is never allowed / not an absolute folder path: write it from the drive letter (C:\…) or from / (/home/…) | `options-model.ts` `UPLOAD_ROOT_REASON`, `UPLOAD_RELATIVE_REASON` |
| K-opt-sum | summary | `No upload folder: every upload is refused.` or `<n> upload folder.` / `<n> upload folders.`, then ` <k> invalid line is not used (marked below).` / ` <k> invalid lines are not used (marked below).` | `options-model.ts` `analyzeUploadFolders` |
| K-opt-item | list item | `Line <n>: “<text>” — <reason>` | `extension/src/options.ts` `renderFolders` |
| K-caps | caps list item | `Line <n>: a very long site name with a port or *. may make a rule Chrome rejects; while it is saved, agent navigations and downloads may be refused.` (`Lines <n>, <m>: …` for several) | `options-model.ts` `blockListLongHostWarning` (executed 2026-10-27 on the three hosts below) |
| K-fetch | `INTERNAL` | `Failed to fetch` (Chrome's text for a refused connection; today's answer: a plain `Error` becomes `INTERNAL` with its message) | `extension/src/errors.ts` (`toWire`), `handlers/download.ts` (rethrows the fetch error) |
| K-newtab-late | `INTERNAL` | `Timed out waiting for tab <id> to load` (only if the page never completes) | `extension/src/tabs.ts` `waitForLoad` |

*K-gate, K-notarget, K-devt, K-disarmed, K-raw-* and K-opt-sum/-item/-root/-rel are the same text as §4.23's table; K-opt-stand and K-opt-home are the S0F-a texts, K-raw-* the S0F-b texts, K-url as a download answer the S3b mapping. If a constant a helper reads differs from its text here, that is a STOP before liveA, never an update of the table.*

**Long-host fixtures (RD-CAPS-WARN, RD-REGEX).** Three hosts of three dot-separated labels of the letter `a`, each with `:8085`: **H119** (labels of 24, 24 and 22) gives a regex of 119 characters, **H120** (24, 24, 23) 120, **H121** (24, 24, 24) 121 (the regex is `^[^:/]+://(?:[^/?#]*[@.])?<host>\.?:8085(?:[/?#]|$)`, 47 + host length for a host of three labels; the 119 bound is `MAX_SAFE_REGEX_CHARS`, #300). `blockListRuleWarnings` was run on them: H119 alone → no warning; H119 then H120 → one item, `Line 2`; H119, H120, H121 → `Lines 2, 3`.

| RD-DL-HOP (S3b, #303; RECORD of order and latency) | A, G1 | List as RD-1; `download-file http://localhost:8083/dl/hello.txt?s11b=dlhop<i> --file hop<i>.txt` for i = 1, 2, 3, one after the other; `LAT` each; hits per tag | Each: K-url, no `data`, no file; `HITS dlhop<i> redirect=1 blocked=0`; `LAT RD-DL-HOP-<i> <ms> ms` | Judged per trial: exact code and message, no `data`, no file, blocked = 0, `RULES 0` after. **RECORD, not judged:** the three times, `d` as defined below, the three RD-DL-ERR times and RD-3's. The event/rejection order is not observable (no log site may be added, 0.8), so the reading is stated now and only recorded. RD-DL-ERR gets no blocked-request event, so its reply always waits the full 300 ms (`settle()` in `safety-hop.ts` returns at once when a hit is recorded, wakes early on a mark, else runs the timer). So `d` = (median of the three RD-DL-HOP times) minus (median of the three RD-DL-ERR times): `d` about -300 ms means the event was in hand at the rejection; `d` within about 50 ms of 0 means the event came late in the 300 ms window, which is the "revisit if" trigger of #303 (latency near 300 ms); a positive `d` beyond 50 ms is unexplained and recorded as such. | `R17 rd-dl-hop` → `S9 call … download-file`, `B17 hits` |

*Dated note, 2026-10-27, before any Run 17 observation (decisions.md #306; S4-live substep 0). RD-LOGS gains one category, `chrome-blocked=<n>`: a line of the worker console capture (the console stream only, never `daemon.log` or an actor's result) that holds Chrome's own `net::ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT` network-error form for a request DNR blocked, is not a `[bridge]` line, and names the BLK fixture origin `localhost:8085` and nothing else that counts (no other pattern, no non-fixture URL, no `127.0.0.1`). Such a line is counted as `chrome-blocked=<n>`, appended to the SCAN line only when n > 0, reported, and is not a STOP; it is left out of the other counts. Every other occurrence of a pattern stays counted and a STOP as the row says: any `[bridge]` line (also one holding `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT`), `daemon.log`, an actor's result, any other console line, and a Chrome line that names another pattern or a non-fixture URL. Committed evidence holds a Chrome blocked-request line only as the label `[chrome-blocked line redacted]` (`B17 redact-console`). The row's text above is unchanged.*

**Placeholder rows → rows above.** *Merged:* RD-PERM and RD-START into RD-0 (a), (b); RD-NAV-HOP into RD-1 (same call); RD-REBUILD into RD-5b (the in-flight count and 0 hits are one measurement). *Kept, reworded:* RD-BACK, RD-FWD, RD-BACK-SAMEDOC, RD-BACK-EMPTY, RD-DISARM, RD-DL-HOP, RD-DL-ERR, RD-DL-SOCKET, RD-REBUILD-NARROW, RD-CAPS-WARN, F-RAW, F-RAW-2, F-RAW-DIR, F-RAW-ENC, F-RAW-OK. *Renamed:* Run 16's UP-L1, UP-6, UP-7, SAVE-REFUSE-R, UP-OPT, UP-RACE-A, UP-DEVT → the `R17-` ids. *Added (0.10, Stage 0):* RD-CTL, RD-1 … RD-8, RD-5b, RD-REGEX, F-HOME, F-FILETAB, RD-0, RD-RESTORE, RD-LOGS. *Dropped:* none. D4.3's rows RD-1, RD-2, RD-3 show 0 blocked hits (RD-2 is a RECORD, #295, so its blocked count is recorded, not judged), RD-4 shows 1, RD-6 shows 1.

*Known limits, recorded before the run (not failures):* the worker console's expression entry by UIA is unmeasured on Chrome 156 (RD-0's fallback names the consequence); a 302 makes the history entry the final URL, so RD-BACK, RD-FWD need the flip server; the order of `onErrorOccurred` and the fetch rejection is not observable (RD-DL-HOP records times only); the rule-count cap and a home line already in storage are unit-tested only; `chrome.runtime.getManifest()` shows the manifest the loaded build has, not that the extensions page was reloaded.

**Commands S4-helpers must provide** (names are the contract; every new file is in S4's lane, `recipes/s11b*` and `recipes/test/s11b-*`, with no exception (`recipes/s9-redirect.py` is not changed); Run 16's `s11.ts`, `s11-win.ps1` and `s11-r16.ps1` are not edited; a `tsconfig.s11b.json` and `s11b-*` tests cover the pure rules, an expected text compared with its constant by name). `CTX <label> count=<n>` joins the printed line forms of the abbreviations paragraph. Each item names the file it touches:
1. `s11b-r17 (.ps1)` (new): `-Block <id>` and `liveA`/`liveB` (all rows, prologue blocks `preflight servers files window console reload teardown`); one stop procedure as `s11-r16.ps1`; dot-sources `s9-win.ps1`, `s11-win.ps1` and `s11b-win.ps1`.
2. `s11b (.ts)` (new): `B17 manifest`, `B17 rules [--sample <ms> --for <ms>]`, `B17 regex` (fragment builder, no pattern literal, pre-type pattern check, hash against `rulesOf`; RD-REGEX), `B17 ctx` (RD-7's `CTX` line), `B17 stored` (RD-5's read-back of `storage.local` by count and an in-process hash match; prints only the verdict). Their UIA console entry is `Invoke-S11bConsoleEval` in `s11b-win (.ps1)` (new): evaluate one fixed expression in the worker's DevTools console and print only `MANIFEST …`, `RULES …`, `REGEX …`, `CTX …`, `STORED …`; the sampler is an in-console interval, not product code.
3. `s11b (.ts)`: `B17 hits --log <file> --tag <t>` (`HITS` line, by query tag) and `B17 ctl` (RD-CTL's counters and the flip check): RD-CTL, RD-1 … RD-8, RD-DL-*, RD-BACK, RD-FWD, RD-5, RD-5b, RD-DISARM.
4. Servers: `s11b-delay (.py)` (new, 8086: sleeps the given seconds, then 302 to the given target; `s9-redirect.py` stays untouched); `s11b-flip (.py)` (new, 8091); `s11b-slow (.py)` (new, 8087); tests in `recipes/test/s11b-*`: RD-5b, RD-DISARM, RD-REBUILD-NARROW, RD-DL-SOCKET, RD-BACK, RD-FWD.
5. `s11b (.ts)`: `B17 back`, `B17 forward` (the SDK's history calls, `LAT` printed) and `B17 socket` (extensionConnected sampler): RD-BACK, RD-FWD, RD-BACK-SAMEDOC, RD-BACK-EMPTY, RD-DL-SOCKET.
6. `s11b-win (.ps1)`: `Open-S11bWindow` (a window opener whose allow-list admits the sheet's fixture ports 8083 and 8081 and nothing else; `Open-S11Window` admits only 8081 and 8084 and is not edited: RD-6), `Get-S11bCaps` (reads the block-list page's `#blocklist-caps` items) and `Open-S11bFileWindow` (a `file:` page for the wave-made file; `Open-S11Window` in `s11-win.ps1` stays allow-listed against `file:`): RD-CAPS-WARN, F-FILETAB.
7. `s11b (.ts)`: `B17 race` paced (150 ms) with `CALL <n> …` lines, `B17 devt` with the control `evaluate` calls, `B17 up6` with the no-other-call sampler window, `B17 raw` expecting K-raw-*; they reuse Run 16's `s11.ts` helpers by import, never edit them: R17-UP-RACE-A, R17-UP-DEVT, R17-UP-6, F-RAW*.

### 4.25 R17B rows (Step 11b S7-sheet, Run 17b: Run 17's FAIL and NOT RUN rows again, RD-2 on the S6 build, the back rows with an activation)

*Written 2026-10-27 by Step 11b slice S7-sheet (Claude Sonnet 5.5), before any Run 17b row ran and before the S6 build was loaded (exit checks D7.1, D7.2). No observation was made to write it; Chrome was not touched. Sources: phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-26-step11b-block-list-redirects.md` § S7, § Exit checks D7.1–D7.7, § STOP rules (the #311 paragraph); decisions.md #308, #309, #311, #313, #314; `…-steps/S4-run-record.md` (which Run 17 rows were FAIL or NOT RUN); `S4-investigation.md` (what made `back` work); `S6.md` § For S7-sheet. §4.24 and Run 17's §5 record are not edited. A row marked "as R17 `<id>`" has the steps, expected text and pass rule of that §4.24 row word for word, with the one change named beside it; every `K-…` key is §4.24's table K17 (unchanged, still the single source of expected text). The ids are new (`R17B-…`); a re-measurement is a new row, never an edit (agent-rules §2).*

**Pass rules, written before any observation.** The preamble of §4.24 applies unchanged: a row passes when every clause of its pass rule holds and each quoted text matches character for character; `RECORD` passes by being recorded; `NOT MEASURED` never counts; an `ok` is never evidence (the evidence is a code and message, a per-tag server-log count, a rule count, the page's own reporter, a UIA read); hits are counted per query tag (`s11b=<tag>`); a failed row is recorded and the wave goes on; nothing is re-run or re-tuned in the wave; nothing the preamble lists as never printed is printed (no token, no list text, no rule, no profile path, no non-fixture URL, no screenshot). Fixtures on 127.0.0.1 and `localhost` only (G11b-2).

**The activation (named before the run, #308).** Chrome's `goBack` and `goForward` skip a history entry whose page was left with no user activation (measured 26 of 26 refused without one, 8 of 8 `ok` with one: `S4-investigation.md` §3 A3). The method is **the bridge's own `evaluate` (`browser_evaluate`, the SDK's `Tab.evaluate`) of the expression `1`**, sent on each page of a history right after the page loaded and before the tab leaves it (the last page too). The code: `extension/src/debugger.ts:186` sends CDP `Runtime.evaluate` with `userGesture: true` (also `extension/src/evaluate/save.ts:104`), the call the investigation used. Preconditions: (1) the JavaScript switch is ON (`evaluate` is class `evaluate`; R17B-0 (c) already stops the wave unless armed with JavaScript on); (2) the tab is not blocked and not a `file:` page; (3) the call attaches `chrome.debugger` for its duration, so Chrome's "extension is debugging this browser" banner shows on the wave's own tab for those calls (the same consequence as every `evaluate`); no banner-sampling row runs near one, and R17B-UP-6's window allows no other bridge call. **Unmeasured gap, stated now:** the investigation sent `Runtime.evaluate` over its own CDP connection; the bridge sends the same method and parameters through `chrome.debugger` and detaches before the next call. R17B-ACT-CTL measures that. If it refuses, the alternative to propose (not used in this wave, no invention) is a trusted click on a fixture link (`browser_click {trusted:true}`, a real CDP `Input` event; the investigation measured a user-activated link click `ok` 2 of 2, but that builds the history by link navigation, not by `navigate`, so it is a different set-up and a new run).
**Activation rule (also a STOP line).** An activation is given **only on a fixture page the wave opened** (an 8081 page or an 8091 flip page in a tab the helper created; no other port needs one); the helper checks the tab's handle is in its own `opened` list and the URL's origin is port 8081 or 8091 before each activation, and prints `STOP: activation target is not a wave fixture page` otherwise (a unit-tested rule of S7-helpers). Never on the human's tabs, on `chrome://` or `file:` pages, or on the options window.

**STOP rule (#193), #311 added.** As §4.24's, binding Run 17b in full. Also a STOP: R17B-RD-2 or any other covered call showing one blocked hit (`blocked` > 0); `newTab` opening its URL before its rules are installed, or a blank tab left after a refused install; an activation on anything but a wave fixture page; **a `back` or `forward` refused on an activated set-up on a page with no redirect** (R17B-ACT-CTL, any set-up `back` of R17B-FWD, R17B-FWD's first `forward` P0 → P1, R17B-BACK-SAMEDOC's `back`, R17B-BACK-EMPTY; R17B-BACK and R17B-FWD's second `forward` land on a flip URL that now answers 302, so they keep RD-BACK's FAIL-and-continue rule): the #311 reading of RD-BACK-EMPTY's clause; a set-up `back` is never turned into "NOT BUILT" (Run 17's helper fix 5 is not carried into the R17B blocks). `R17B-BACK-NOACT` is a RECORD row and cannot fire it. A helper may be fixed only between rows, never past a STOP line.

**Fixtures and ports.** `netstat -ano` on 2026-10-27 04:55 UTC (this slice; 284 lines; filtered for local ports 8080–8099 and 9234: no line, exit 1): 8080–8099 and 9234 free, no daemon running. Listening for other programs and not used by this sheet: 9180, 9222 (and system ports). 8080 stays unused (#273). The preflight re-checks every port below and stops on a holder the wave did not start. Servers as §4.24's table (same commands, logs outside the repo): **8081** allowed pages and history pages; **8083** `s9-redirect.py` (RD-2); **8085** the blocked fixture, **BLK** = `localhost:8085`; **8086** `s11b-delay` (R17B-DISARM); **8087** `s11b-slow` (R17B-NEWTAB-LOAD, R17B-RD-DL-SOCKET); **8091** `s11b-flip` (the back rows). 8088 and 8089 (RD-7, RD-8) and 8090 are not started: those rows ran in Run 17 and are not repeated.

**Slices and order.** **liveA17B** then **liveB17B** (only with "Allow access to file URLs" ON, read by R17B-0 as §4.23's UP-0, never pressed; OFF → the seven liveB rows are written `NOT RUN — waits for G11-3` and liveB17B does not start, as §4.24; an unreadable toggle is a STOP). Prologue (ledger, not rows): preflight, servers, files (`<ROOT>`, `<OUTSIDE>`, the folder and the `ü` file), window, **console** (the worker DevTools window opened and captured before R17B-0, kept to R17B-LOGS), `npm run build` from a clean tree at a commit that has S6, reload by `recipes/reload-extension.ps1` (#65, #99). Order of liveA17B: **R17B-0**, R17B-UP-L1, R17B-CTL, **R17B-ACT-CTL**, R17B-BACK-NOACT; then the helper builds the activated histories for R17B-BACK, R17B-FWD and R17B-BACK-SAMEDOC with the list as found; then **group G1** (list = found + BLK, saved once, read back by count and hash): R17B-RD-2, R17B-NEWTAB-PLAIN, R17B-NEWTAB-LOAD, R17B-BACK, R17B-FWD, R17B-BACK-SAMEDOC, R17B-DISARM; list restored (read back); then R17B-NEWTAB-REFUSED, R17B-BACK-EMPTY, R17B-RD-DL-SOCKET, R17B-UP-OPT, R17B-UP-6, R17B-UP-7, R17B-SAVE-REFUSE; then R17B-RESTORE and R17B-LOGS. liveB17B: R17B-0's found record again, ROOT added as R17B-UP-OPT does, the F-RAW rows, R17B-UP-RACE-A, R17B-UP-DEVT, R17B-RESTORE, R17B-LOGS. A row that changes the safety state restores it before the next group. Only windows the wave opened are touched; the toolbar button must be pinned (R17B-DISARM, R17B-UP-7, R17B-UP-RACE-A; unpinned is a STOP, never a mouse click).

**Helper abbreviations.** `R17B <block>` = `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\recipes\s11b-r17b.ps1 -Block <block>` (new; `r17b-…` blocks, `liveA17B`/`liveB17B`). `B17`, `S9`, `S11` as §4.24. New line forms: `BUILD disk-s6-names=<n>/2 created-url=<about:blank|other>`, `TABWATCH created-url=<about:blank|other> completes=<n> first-complete-ms=<ms> reply-ms=<ms>`, `ACT <label> n=<k> ok`, `HIST <label> built=<n> pages activated=<n>`.

Not repeated, because they passed or were recorded in Run 17: RD-0 of Run 17 itself (R17B-0 is its successor), RD-CTL (R17B-CTL is a narrowed RD-CTL: 8083, 8086, 8091, the 8085 counter and 8087; 8088 and 8089 are not started), F-FILETAB, F-HOME, RD-CAPS-WARN, RD-REGEX, RD-1, RD-6, RD-3, RD-DL-ERR, RD-DL-HOP, RD-7, RD-8, RD-REBUILD-NARROW, RD-4, RD-5, RD-5b.

**Helpers needed (S7-helpers; every new file in `recipes/s11b*` and `recipes/test/s11b-*`; Run 17's `s11b-r17.ps1`, its sheet and the existing ops' behaviour are not edited):** (1) `s11b-r17b.ps1` (new): the `r17b-…` blocks above and `liveA17B`/`liveB17B`, dot-sourcing the existing helpers; it prints R17B-0's first line (the `MANIFEST` text from `B17 manifest` joined to the `BUILD` text from `B17 build-proof`, one line, printed by this block) and has no "NOT BUILT" path for a set-up `back`. (2) `s11b.ts`: new ops `B17 build-proof` (disk name count, ancestor check, and the `created-url` from the tabwatch read), `B17 tabwatch` (start/read the in-console `chrome.tabs.onCreated`/`onUpdated` listener; labels and numbers only), `B17 newtab-timed` (`LAT` of the reply, then the snapshot); a **new history kind** for three 8081 pages (`HistoryKind` and `historyPlan` in `s11b-rules.ts`; R17B-ACT-CTL, R17B-BACK-NOACT); a flag `--activate` on `B17 history` (an `evaluate 1` after each load, the wave-fixture-page guard, the `HIST` line) **under which a set-up `back` refusal is a STOP and the `histNotBuilt17`/`bump(3)` NOT BUILT path (`s11b.ts` ~720-735, Run 17's fix 5) is disabled**; the R17B judges print a STOP line for a non-`ok` answer of R17B-ACT-CTL, R17B-FWD's first `forward`, R17B-BACK-SAMEDOC and R17B-BACK-EMPTY, including the first call of a `--times 2` pair (the existing `historyCall` turns that into a plain row FAIL, `s11b.ts` ~690, and the RD-BACK-EMPTY judge returns FAIL, ~701); the R17B row ids added to `HISTORY_ROWS` with their judges, so `B17 back`/`B17 forward` print `ROW R17B-…` lines, not `ROW RD-…`; the timed 8087 GET in `B17 ctl` (or a new op) for `CTL 8087 slow answered after <ms> ms`. `B17 hits`, `B17 rules` and `S9 call … newtab` are reused unchanged. (3) `s11b-win.ps1`: `Invoke-S11bPressAfterReply` (ON timed from the reply; the first press unchanged) and the console op `tabwatch` of `Invoke-S11bConsoleEval`. (4) Tests: the activation target guard (8081 and 8091 only), the `BUILD` line parser, the `TABWATCH` judge, the `--activate` STOP in place of NOT BUILT, the STOP line for a refused target call (including the first of a `--times 2` pair), the press-after-reply timing (a fake clock), and each new expected text compared with this table by key.

*Known limits, recorded before the run:* the investigation measured activation by `Runtime.evaluate` over its own CDP link, not through `chrome.debugger` (R17B-ACT-CTL); the N1 cases of S6 are measured only as they occur (nothing forces a blank tab to stall); `tabCreated.url` is read from Chrome's own event, not from the extension's redacted frame (the daemon holds that one and no client sees it); a `back` that reaches a history the bridge built alone stays Step 12's (#308 (b)).

### 4.26 R18 rows (Step 12 S1-sheet, Run 18: the helper-fix wave — Run 17b's FAIL and NOT MEASURED upload rows again, with a console capture per slice, a banner-sampler control, UP-RACE-A and UP-OPT re-written)

*Written 2026-10-27 by Step 12 slice S1-sheet (Claude Sonnet 5.5, medium), before any Run 18 row ran, before the S1 helpers were built and before a Run 18 build was loaded (exit checks R1.1 and the sheet half of R1.2–R1.7). No observation was made to write it: no Chrome, no daemon, no page; the only commands run were `netstat -ano`, `date -u`, `Get-NetTCPConnection` and `Get-Date` (Fixtures and ports). Sources: phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-27-step12-phase-f.md` § 0.3 and the dated note under § Stage 0 (G-21–G-24), § S1, § Exit checks R1.1–R1.7, § Human gates, § STOP rules and forbidden fixes; `…-step12-phase-f-steps/Stage0.md` §6 and `stage0-audit-2.md` §3, §5; `docs/plans/2026-10-26-step11b-block-list-redirects-steps/S7-run-record.md` §1 and §5; decisions #320, #329, #339; `recipes/CLAUDE.md` (Run 17b's helper facts); `env-checker.md`. §4.23, §4.24, §4.25 and the Run 16, 17 and 17b records in §5 are not edited. The ids are new (`R18-…`); a re-measurement is a new row, never an edit (agent-rules §2).*

**The "as" convention and the two re-writes.** A row marked "as R17B `<id>`" has the steps, expected text and pass rule of that §4.25 row, and where that row says "as R17 `<id>`" (or "As §4.23 `<ID>`") the §4.24 row (and, where it points on, the §4.23 row with §4.24's stated S0F-a expectations) **word for word**, with only the changes named beside it in the table below. Every `K-…` key is §4.24's table K17 (K-gate, K-notarget, K-devt, K-undef, K-node, K-map, K-cycle, K-raw-missing, K-raw-dir, K-opt-*) and §4.23's; neither is edited. The one-line map (source row → line in this file at writing time): R17B-0 1701; R17B-UP-L1 1702 → R17-UP-L1 1629 → UP-L1 1512; R17-UP-OPT 1653 → UP-OPT 1514; R17-UP-6 1654 → UP-6 1516; R17-SAVE-REFUSE 1656 → SAVE-REFUSE-R 1518; F-RAW 1657, F-RAW-2 1658, F-RAW-DIR 1659; R17-UP-RACE-A 1662 → UP-RACE-A 1535; R17-UP-DEVT 1663 → UP-DEVT 1532; RD-RESTORE 1664; RD-LOGS 1665 with its dated note 1667. **Only #320's two re-writes change a carried pass rule: R18-UP-RACE-A (the ON press timed from the last call's reply, as RD-DISARM's fix #308) and R18-UP-OPT (the O2 poll runs to 15 s and records whether and when the saved text showed; the bound stays 5 000 ms).** Every other difference in this section is a new row (R18-0, R18-BANNER-CTL), the id, or a consequence #320 or the phase doc's note states (named in the row's cell), never a loosened or tightened pass rule.

**Pass rules, written before any observation.** The preambles of §4.23, §4.24 and §4.25 apply unchanged: a row passes when every clause of its pass rule holds and each quoted text matches character for character; `RECORD` passes by being recorded; `NOT MEASURED` never counts as a pass; **an `ok` is never evidence** (the evidence is a code and message, the page's own reporter, a MutationObserver record of `data-__bridge-upload`, a UIA read, a count); a failed row is recorded and the wave goes on; nothing is re-run, re-tuned or fixed in the wave; nothing those preambles list as never printed is printed (no token, no folder-box or block-list text, no path under the user's profile (`<ROOT>\name`, `<OUTSIDE>\name`), no non-fixture URL or title, no screenshot, no expression, no `saveTo` path). A row whose only unmet clause is a banner clause made NOT MEASURED by a failed R18-BANNER-CTL has the verdict NOT MEASURED (the clause named); a row with any other clause failed is FAIL. A carried row that fails **with a passing control** is a product finding: recorded, proposed, not a STOP (phase doc § STOP rules, "Continues anyway"). The controls are: R18-BANNER-CTL for the banner clauses; the K-devt control call for R18-UP-DEVT; SAVE-REFUSE's `boom` control; UP-RACE-A's "at least one `ok` and one refusal" discrimination test; R18-UP-L1's `FOUNDCHECK`. Fixtures on 127.0.0.1 and `localhost` only (G12-3).

**STOP rule (#193), the phase doc's added.** On any STOP line (a failed restore; a guard printing STOP; a human gate such as an unpinned toolbar button; a port held by a program the wave did not start; an unreadable file-URL toggle; a found state that is not armed with JavaScript on; a tracked file changed outside the steps dir; any throw after the first state change) the runner restores first if the safety state was touched, records the read-back by count and hash, writes the ledger and returns. Also a STOP (phase doc § STOP rules, those that bind Run 18): a **native dialog left open** on the user's screen (a dialog the wave causes is closed by the wave); a **DevTools window left open** (the sheet's own guard; DevTools is opened only in window X); a **`CLAUDE.md` runtime invariant that would have to change**; a **safety state not restored**; a **file reaching a page from outside the Upload folders** (any carried upload row that answers `ok` for a path outside ROOT where K-gate is written, or a reporter holding a file taken from `<OUTSIDE>` or from outside ROOT; a reporter holding a file from inside ROOT where 0 is written is a FAIL of its row, a "continues anyway" finding, not a STOP); **console text, an `args` value, a path, a pattern, a URL or the token** in a log or in committed evidence (a sentinel counted, never quoted beyond its fixture string); a **port-9234 holder that is not the daemon**, or an orphan process; **anything pushed or tagged**; a pre-registered check that fails after the allowed fix rounds (the gate stops; the write-up continues). **Forbidden fixes** (phase doc): editing a row's expected text after its run; a pass rule of Runs 16–17b changed beyond #320's two re-writes; a new error code or a changed message to make a row pass; and every forbidden fix of Steps 9–11b (`docs/agent-rules.md` §2). A helper may be fixed only between rows, never past a STOP line. Every STOP or guard line printed is matched to a return in the ledger (R1.7).

**Human gates and windows.** Only windows the wave opened are touched (the human's tab is the wave's own `W` tab). State changes go through the options page in the wave's own window or the toolbar button (pinned; an unpinned button is a STOP, never a mouse click). The Upload folders box is edited only in the wave's own options window and restored exactly. **The file-URL toggle ("Allow access to file URLs") is read, never pressed** (G11-3 as in Steps 11 and 11b). DevTools is opened only in window X the wave opened on a fixture page. No Chrome restart; the reload is by `recipes/reload-extension.ps1` (#65, one re-run on exit 2, #99). Fixture pages only: no real site, no credentials, no profile path in evidence. The block list is not edited in Run 18.

**Fixtures and ports.** `netstat -ano` at **2026-10-27 15:21 UTC** (`date -u` printed `Tue, Oct 27, 2026 3:21:44 PM`; PowerShell `(Get-Date).ToUniversalTime()` 15:21:48; this slice; 296 lines; filtered for local ports 8080–8099 and 9234: no line, grep exit 1): 8080–8099 and 9234 free, no daemon running. Listening for other programs and not used: 9180 (PID 5168), 9222 (PIDs 2528 and 30380). `Get-NetTCPConnection` shows one **Bound** entry on **8082** (PID 25896, not listening, not in `netstat`; the same PID `env-checker.md` saw): **8082 is avoided**. 8080 stays unused (#273). **Chosen: 8081 only**, `python -m http.server 8081 --bind 127.0.0.1 --directory test-fixtures` started by the wave (stdout/stderr to a log outside the repo): the pages `s11-upload.html` (+ `s11-upload.js`) for the upload rows and `e2e-playground.html` for the control tab. No carried row needs a second server (no redirect, block-list, CSP or alias row runs), so no other port is taken. The preflight re-checks 8081 and 9234 and stops on a holder the wave did not start. A URL counts as **fixture** only if `http://` on `127.0.0.1:8081` or `localhost:8081` (and `*.localhost:8081`), `chrome://extensions`, or this extension's own `chrome-extension://<id>/` pages; any other `scheme://host`, another loopback port included, counts as other (§4.23's UP-LOGS note, 8084 dropped because no 8084 server runs).

**Slices and order (#271).** **liveA18** then **liveB18** (liveB only with "Allow access to file URLs" ON, read by R18-0 as §4.23's UP-0, never pressed; OFF → R18-F-RAW, -F-RAW-2, -F-RAW-DIR, R18-UP-RACE-A and R18-UP-DEVT are written `NOT RUN — waits for G11-3` and liveB18 does not start, as §4.24 and §4.25; an unreadable toggle is a STOP). Prologue (ledger, not rows): preflight, servers, files (`<ROOT>`, `<OUTSIDE>` as `<ROOT>`'s sibling `outside`, `s11-1k.bin`, `s11-sent.bin`, `s11-out.bin`, a folder inside ROOT for F-RAW-DIR, the sentinel folder; the files the carried rows name, made as in Run 17b), window, **the slice's own console capture started before R18-0**, `npm run build` from a clean tree at a committed HEAD, reload by `recipes/reload-extension.ps1`, then R18-0. Order of **liveA18**: **R18-0**, **R18-UP-L1** (before any state change), **R18-BANNER-CTL**, **R18-UP-OPT** (the wave's first Upload-folders change; ROOT stays added until R18-RESTORE), **R18-UP-6**, **R18-SAVE-REFUSE**, then **R18-RESTORE** and **R18-LOGS**. Order of **liveB18**: R18-0's found record again, ROOT added as R18-UP-OPT does (no new row), **R18-BANNER-CTL** (liveB's own), **R18-F-RAW**, **R18-F-RAW-2**, **R18-F-RAW-DIR**, **R18-UP-RACE-A**, **R18-UP-DEVT**, then **R18-RESTORE** and **R18-LOGS**. A row that changes the safety state restores it before the next row. Runner attempts: at most three (phase doc § S1; §7's fix rounds apply).

**The console capture, one file per slice (#320 item 1; R1.2).** The worker's DevTools window is opened on the extension's service worker and its console captured, **for each slice to its own file**: raw, in the wave's out folder outside the repo, `console-liveA18.txt` and `console-liveB18.txt`; after a passing R18-LOGS the redacted copy is committed to the steps dir as `S1-live-console-liveA18.txt` and `S1-live-console-liveB18.txt` (an attempt after the first adds `-att2`, `-att3`). liveA's file is closed (never written again) before liveB's capture opens. The helper's unit-tested guards: the two paths differ; a capture path that already holds text is a STOP (an overwrite is how Run 17b lost liveA's). The **control line** is the toolbar's disarm badge title, exact text **`safety: disarmed by the user`** (`extension/src/badge.ts` `disarmed`), written by the control press of R18-RESTORE (off, at least 1.5 s, on) — and by R18-UP-RACE-A's press in liveB. **Expected text for each slice's capture:** the file holds that line at least once (`control-disarmed=<n>` with n ≥ 1) and nothing of it is quoted beyond the line itself. The record quotes the control line **from that slice's own file** (R1.2). A slice whose file lacks the line reads R18-LOGS NOT MEASURED for that slice, whatever the helper's judge prints.

**R18-BANNER-CTL and what it decides (#320 item 1; G-21).** Why: Run 17b's sampler read `banner=seen` on legs that send no call and attach no debugger (R17B-UP-6 `missing`, `relative`), so a positive reading proved nothing. The control runs in **each slice before that slice's first banner row** (liveA: before R18-UP-6; liveB: before R18-F-RAW), on a tab the wave opened (`http://localhost:8081/e2e-playground.html?s12=bc`), the sampler being the one the banner rows use, with the same lead and tail (**500 ms before the step to 2 500 ms after its end**, as R17-UP-6 writes it) and no other bridge call in flight. **Negative leg (first):** the sampler's full window with **no bridge call at all** (the "step" is empty), started **at least 15 s after the last attach** (the last reply of any call that can attach `chrome.debugger`: an `evaluate`, `evaluateRef`, `evaluateToFile`, a trusted input, an upload that passed its refusals, a screenshot with a debugger step; G-21 raises Run 13 SAFE-4 (b)'s linger bound of 14 s to 15 s); the helper waits for it and prints the idle it measured. **Positive leg (second):** the same window around **one `evaluate`** of the expression `1` on that tab. **Pass rule:** negative `banner=none` **and** positive `banner=seen` **and** the positive's `evaluate` answered `ok` **and** the negative leg's `idle-ms` ≥ 15 000. Anything else is FAIL: `banner=seen` on the negative leg means the sampler cannot discriminate, `none` on the positive that it cannot see the banner. **Written before the run: a row's banner clause is judged by the helper on its own slice's R18-BANNER-CTL (a slice whose control did not run reads its own banner clauses NOT MEASURED; a slice that did not start changes nothing). The run-wide rule is applied by S1-record: if the control fails in either slice, every banner clause of the run (R18-UP-6, R18-F-RAW, -F-RAW-2, -F-RAW-DIR, both slices) is NOT MEASURED and is recorded so; the other clauses of those rows are judged as written.** After the positive leg the banner lingers 3–14 s (Run 13 SAFE-4 (b)): **every banner row's first step starts at least 15 s after the last attach**, the helper prints `IDLE <row> since-attach-ms=<n>`, and an `IDLE` under 15 000 ms is a helper STOP printed before the row's first call (a guard of the helper, no new NOT MEASURED clause on a carried rule). F-RAW legs answer before any attach (G-24, `handlers/upload.ts` order), so the right words for the sampler's question are "attach no debugger", not "send no call".

**Line forms the helpers print (the contract to S1-helpers-a and S1-helpers-b; the pass rules above and below read them; unit tests compare each form with this section by key).** Existing, unchanged: `SAMPLER <row> polls=<n> (after the reply <p>) banner=<seen|none>`; `ROW <id> <STOP|FAIL|NOT DISCRIMINATING|NOT MEASURED|NOT RUN|PASS|RECORD>[ …]`; `CHECK …`; `ANSWER <row> <code|ok> …`; `LEAKS <row> <n>`; `RACE P=<ms> from ready; calls=<n>`; `CALL <n> start=<ms> reply=<ms> <ok|CODE>`; `KEY F12 pressed into window …`; `SCAN <what>: ://=<n> (fixture <n>, other <n>) token=<n> sentinel=<n> profile=<n>`; `RESTORE …`; `RULES <n>`; R17B-0's first line and `STATE`/`TOGGLE`/`FOUNDLIST`/`S6` lines. **New (S1-helpers-a):** (1) `CONSOLE <slice> capture=<file name only> texts=<n> control-disarmed=<n>`, printed after the slice's R18-RESTORE, `<slice>` = `liveA18`|`liveB18` (and `slice=<A|B>` is the same slice's letter); (2) `BANNERCTL <slice> leg=neg idle-ms=<n> polls=<n> banner=<seen|none>` and `BANNERCTL <slice> leg=pos answer=<ok|CODE> polls=<n> banner=<seen|none>`, then `ROW R18-BANNER-CTL <PASS|FAIL> slice=<A|B>`; (3) `IDLE <row> since-attach-ms=<n>`; (4) `DEVT <slice> control1 <ok|CODE> attached=<yes|no>` (`attached=yes` = control 1 answered `DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS`) and `DEVT <slice> control2 <ok|CODE>` around the upload, the DevTools-opening method being the helper's (G-22: F12 did not attach in Run 17b; the row is judged only by control 1's K-devt). **New (S1-helpers-b):** (5) `RAW <row> <label> code=<CODE|ok> key=<K-…|other|none> data=<none|keys:<k1,k2>>` (data keys only, never values) for each R18-UP-L1 raw call (the codes, not only the checks); (6) `OPT observed standing x<n> invalid items <fi+3 when the found folders hold fi invalid lines> warned items <n>` computed from §4.24's S0F-a texts and the three typed lines (the stale `$S11Warning` and its two-line regex of `s11-win.ps1:24, 275, 296` are replaced), and `OPT O2 shown=<yes|no> at=<ms|none> polled-ms=<n>`, the poll running to 15 000 ms and stopping when the text matches; (7) `RACE ON pressed <ms> after last reply` beside the existing `RACE` and `CALL` lines; (8) `LEG <row> <leg> <PASS|FAIL|NOT MEASURED>` for a row's legs (SAVE-REFUSE's `sdk-leg`, `mcp-leg`). **Aggregator fixes (S1-helpers-b):** `VERDICT_LINE` accepts a **multi-token row id** (`ROW SAVE-REFUSE-R sdk-leg PASS` counts as one verdict line, not zero; R17B-SAVE-REFUSE printed `NOT MEASURED (0 verdict lines)` because of it); each R18 row prints **exactly one** `ROW <id> <verdict>` line per slice (rows run in both slices end the verdict with `slice=<A|B>`), legs print `LEG`, and a second `ROW` for one id in one slice is a helper fault the aggregator prints as `WARN row printed twice: <id>` and counts once; no interim `NOT MEASURED` text is printed beside a row's PASS (R17B-NEWTAB-LOAD's `blocked or RULES after not given`, G-23); **history set-up lines** (`HIST …`, `ACT …`) go to the record stream, not the framing file (no Run 18 row builds a history, so this is checked by the helper's test, not by a Run 18 row); the scanner counts the extension's own `chrome-extension://<id>/` page URL as fixture (it printed `non-fixture ://` FAILs on `window … now shows` lines, S7-run-record §5 item 8) and `window … now shows` lines print the URL class only.

(g): `tree=clean`, else STOP before any state change; `HEAD` is recorded, and the record states whether HEAD contains Step 12's S2a commit (a note, not judged).
