R1.4: each carried row (table above) against its named source. R1.5: R18-RESTORE per slice. R1.6: R18-LOGS per slice. R1.7: the STOP rule and its ledger match.

**Carried rows, mapped (verbatim except the two re-writes, #320).** R18-UP-L1 ← R17B-UP-L1 (FAIL) ← R17-UP-L1 ← UP-L1: verbatim (id; the `RAW` line added); R18-UP-OPT ← R17B-UP-OPT (FAIL) ← R17-UP-OPT ← UP-OPT: **re-written (poll to 15 s)**; R18-UP-6 ← R17B-UP-6 (FAIL) ← R17-UP-6 ← UP-6: verbatim, the banner clause gated by R18-BANNER-CTL; R18-SAVE-REFUSE ← R17B-SAVE-REFUSE (NOT MEASURED) ← R17-SAVE-REFUSE ← SAVE-REFUSE-R: verbatim, the console consequence written; R18-F-RAW, -F-RAW-2, -F-RAW-DIR ← R17B-F-RAW, -F-RAW-2, -F-RAW-DIR (FAIL) ← F-RAW, F-RAW-2, F-RAW-DIR: verbatim, banner clause gated; R18-UP-RACE-A ← R17B-UP-RACE-A (FAIL) ← R17-UP-RACE-A ← UP-RACE-A: **re-written (ON from the last reply)**; R18-UP-DEVT ← R17B-UP-DEVT (NOT MEASURED) ← R17-UP-DEVT ← UP-DEVT: verbatim, the helper's attach method fixed (G-22); R18-RESTORE ← R17B-RESTORE ← RD-RESTORE: verbatim; R18-LOGS ← R17B-LOGS (NOT MEASURED) ← RD-LOGS: verbatim minus the probes for fixtures that do not run. **Not carried** (they passed in Run 17b; a re-measurement would be a new row if the user wants one): R17B-UP-7, R17B-F-RAW-ENC, R17B-F-RAW-OK, and every redirect, newTab and back/forward row.

**Open items, recorded before the run (not failures; the session agent rules, never a builder):** (1) **Banner window.** R17-UP-6 writes the tail as 2 500 ms after the reply; the helper's `$S11SampleTailMs` is 4 000 ms (`recipes/s11-win.ps1:30`, §4.23's UP-1 rule). This sheet takes the written 2 500 ms for R18-UP-6, R18-F-RAW* and R18-BANNER-CTL; S1-helpers-a sets it per row and the `SAMPLER` line's "after the reply" poll count shows which tail ran. (2) **UP-RACE-A's loop end.** R17 gives "at least 60, at most 120" and no stop condition; the re-write ends the loop at the 120-call cap so that ON comes after every call (the only end the sources fix). A shorter loop needs a rule written before the run. (3) **The build under test.** S2a edits product code in this tree; the phase order puts S1-live before S2a lands, so Run 18 should run at a HEAD without it; R18-0 (g) records HEAD, and the record states what the build contains. (4) **DevTools attach (G-22).** The sources do not say how to make DevTools attach where F12 did not (Run 17b, cause not established); the method is S1-helpers-a's, judged only by K-devt. (5) **Stage0.md §6 names no carried row by id**; the list is the phase doc's 0.3 plus the FAIL and NOT MEASURED verdicts of S7-run-record §1 (R17B-UP-L1, -UP-OPT, -UP-6, -SAVE-REFUSE, -F-RAW, -F-RAW-2, -F-RAW-DIR, -UP-RACE-A, -UP-DEVT, -LOGS). (6) **8082** shows a Windows "Bound" entry without a listener (PID 25896): avoided, not proven taken.

*Known limits, recorded before the run:* the banner's cause on R17B-UP-6 and R17B-F-RAW* is not established; a passing control only licenses reading the banner clause, it does not explain Run 17b's reading; whether DevTools can be made to attach through the helper is unmeasured until the run (R18-UP-DEVT is then NOT MEASURED, as its rule says); a refusal after the CDP call can leave a file in the page (UP-RACE-A's known limit, recorded).

### 4.27 R19 rows (Step 12 S7-sheet, Run 19: the Step 12 feature rows)

*Written 2026-10-28 by Step 12 slice S7-sheet, part A (Claude Sonnet 5.5, medium), before any Run 19 row ran, before the S7 helpers were built and before a Run 19 build was loaded (exit check R7.1, the sheet half of R7.2 and R7.3). No observation was made to write it: no Chrome, no daemon, no page; the only commands run were `netstat -ano`, `date -u` and `Get-NetTCPConnection` (Fixtures and ports). Sources: phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-27-step12-phase-f.md` § 0.4, 0.5, 0.10, 0.11, § S7, § Exit checks R7.1–R7.6, § Human gates, § STOP rules and forbidden fixes, § As built; the Handoff sections of `…-step12-phase-f-steps/S2b.md` (§7), `S3a.md` (§7) and `S3b.md` (§7); the spec's Step 12 S2a and S3a notes (`docs/specs/2026-10-21-daemon-sdk-multi-actor-design.md` §10); decisions #321, #330–#334, #344, #345, #351, #352; `…/S1-run-record.md` §6–§7; §4.26 (the form) and the Run 18 record in §5; `recipes/CLAUDE.md`. §4.23–§4.26 and the Run 16–18 records are not edited. The ids are new (`R19-…`); a re-measurement is a new row, never an edit (agent-rules §2). This is part A of two: part B (the D, E, G2 and carried rows, slices and order, R19-LOGS, R19-RESTORE) is appended after it. Part B (written 2026-10-28 by S7-sheet part B, Claude Sonnet 5.5, medium, again with no observation; commands run: `git show HEAD:<path>`, `git grep`, `git log`, `git status`, `node scripts/doc-check.mjs`) used these further sources: phase doc § 0.6, 0.7, 0.9, 0.10, 0.11, § S4–S7, § Exit checks R7.1–R7.6, § Human gates, § STOP rules and § As built; decisions #337, #339, #349, #351, #352, #353, #354, #355; the Handoff sections and builder reports of `…-step12-phase-f-steps/S3a-builder.md` (§7), `S3b-builder.md` (§7), `S4b.md` and `S4b-builder.md` (§7), `S5a.md` and `S5a-builder.md` (§7), `S5b.md` (§7), `S6-G2.md` and `S6-G2-builder.md` (§7), `S6-G2-verifier.md` (MINOR-4), `S1-run-record.md` §4 and §6; the committed spec §10 notes for S5a and S6-G2 (`git show HEAD:docs/specs/2026-10-21-daemon-sdk-multi-actor-design.md`); committed tests and constants named in the rows; §4.23's key table (K-gate, K-noacc, K-devt, K-disarmed, K-blocked, K-mcp-ok) and §4.24's K17. S5c's work was uncommitted when part B was written; fix round 1 of S7-sheet (S7-sheet-fixer, before any observation) quotes its committed text, from `git show HEAD:<path>` at `e7958eb` (`daemon/src/intercept.ts`, `daemon/test/s12-chooser-feature.test.ts`, the spec §10 S5c note), into R19-VERSION only. The same round applies the verifier's findings (the Banner rule, Capture segments, exceptions in R19-CON-FLOOR, the allowed leg of R19-CON-NOTEA, the new row R19-CLOSER, R19-BANNER-CTL in slices A and C, the split R19-CON-FRAME).*

**Pass rules, written before any observation.** §4.26's preamble applies unchanged: a row passes when every clause of its pass rule holds and each quoted text matches character for character; `RECORD` passes by being recorded; `NOT MEASURED` and `NOT RUN` never count as a pass; **an `ok` is never evidence** (the evidence is a code and message, a count from the page's own reporter, a count of `daemon.log` or the worker console, a banner sampler read with its own control); a failed row is recorded and the wave goes on; nothing is re-run, re-tuned or fixed in the wave. **Nothing the sheet lists as never printed is printed: no sentinel, console text, `args` value, path, URL, title or token** (counts only; the record may quote a fixture's constant label such as `s12=con`, never a reply's text). The sentinel is the fixture constant `SENTINEL` of `s12-console.js`, passed to the scanners by argument. Every expected text below is quoted from the source named beside it. A row that fails is a product finding with its fix a proposed decision, not a STOP, unless a STOP line below names it (phase doc § STOP rules, "Continues anyway").

**STOP rule (#193), §4.26's and the phase doc's added.** §4.26's STOP rule is carried whole (a failed restore; a guard printing STOP; a human gate such as an unpinned toolbar button; a port held by a program the wave did not start; an unreadable file-URL toggle; a found state that is not armed with JavaScript on; a tracked file changed outside the steps dir; any throw after the first state change; a native dialog left open; a DevTools window left open; a `CLAUDE.md` runtime invariant that would have to change; a safety state not restored; a file reaching a page from outside the Upload folders; console text, an `args` value, a path, a pattern, a URL or the token in a log or in committed evidence; a port-9234 holder that is not the daemon, or an orphan process; anything pushed or tagged; a pre-registered check that fails after the allowed fix rounds) and on any STOP line the runner restores first if the safety state was touched, records the read-back by count and hash, writes the ledger and returns. **Added by the phase doc (§ STOP rules), those that bind Run 19:** a **debugger session attached after a disarm**, or **on a tab whose page is blocked, an own page or a `file:` page** (judged only as the **Banner rule** below says: a banner sample positive on a refused call, after `IDLE >= 20 000 ms` and with the slice's R19-BANNER-CTL passing: R19-CON-3, -4, -OWN, -FILE, -JSOFF, R19-ARGS-3, R19-CHOOSE-2, -JSOFF; there is no attach counter and none is built, #351 (2); R19-KEEP-3 and R19-CHOOSE-DISARM carry no banner STOP, see their rows and Open item 5); a **kept session used without the call's gate re-check**; **file-chooser interception still on after its call ended**, or **Chrome's own dialog not opening on CHOOSE-4's human-style click** (part B's rows); an **`evaluate` with `args` running while JavaScript is off** (R19-ARGS-3's effect counter above its control, or a banner positive on its refusal under the Banner rule); an **existing tool answering differently on a call without the new parameters** (R19-ARGS-1's no-`args` control call, R19-CON-MCP's, any carried row: its code or text differs from the source row's); and **every native dialog the wave causes is closed by the wave**. **Forbidden fixes** (phase doc): a console hook in the page without the ruling on Q2; logging console text, `args` or a path; exempting `consoleMessages` from the gate or classing it outside `evaluate`; keeping a session across a disarm; reusing a session without the re-check; intercepting the chooser outside a call; skipping the Upload folders rule, the file-URL check or the missing-file check on the chooser path; building `args` into the expression string in the extension; `String(fn)` of a real function in the SDK; a new error code or a changed message to make a row pass; editing a row's expected text after its run; a pass rule of Runs 16–18 changed; and every forbidden fix of Steps 9–11b (`docs/agent-rules.md` §2). A helper may be fixed only between rows, never past a STOP line. Every STOP or guard line printed is matched to a return in the ledger (R7.6).

**Human gates and windows.** As §4.26's, with the phase doc's § Human gates: only windows the wave opened are touched (the human's tab is the wave's own `W` tab); safety state changes go through the options page in the wave's own window or the toolbar button (pinned; an unpinned button is a STOP, never a mouse click); the block list and Upload folders are edited only in the wave's own options window and restored exactly (**the block list is edited in Run 19**, unlike Run 18: R19-CON-3, -FRAME and -NOTEA add one `*.localhost` pattern and part B's R19-RESTORE reads it back by count and hash); **the file-URL toggle is read, never pressed**; DevTools is opened only in a window the wave opened on a fixture page; no Chrome restart, the reload is by `recipes/reload-extension.ps1` (#65, one re-run on exit 2, #99); **a native dialog the wave causes is closed by the wave**. **G12-3: fixture pages only, on 127.0.0.1 and `localhost` (and `*.localhost`), never a real site or credentials; no profile path in evidence.** A push or tag is G12-1 and not done.

**Fixtures and ports.** `netstat -ano` at **2026-10-28 06:01 UTC** (`date -u` printed `Wed, Oct 28, 2026  6:01:23 AM`; PowerShell `(Get-Date).ToUniversalTime()` 06:01:26; this slice; 326 lines): **8000 is held** (127.0.0.1:8000 LISTENING, PID 27152, not the wave's; avoided); no line for local ports 8001–8099 or 9234 in `netstat`, so 8081 and 9234 are free and no daemon is running; `Get-NetTCPConnection` shows one **Bound** entry on **8082** (PID 25896, no listener; the same PID Run 18 saw): **8082 is avoided**, and 8080 stays unused (#273). **Chosen: 8081 only**, `python -m http.server 8081 --bind 127.0.0.1 --directory test-fixtures` started by the wave (output to a log outside the repo). The preflight re-checks 8081 and 9234 and stops on a holder the wave did not start. A URL counts as **fixture** only if `http://` on `127.0.0.1:8081`, `localhost:8081` or `*.localhost:8081`, `chrome://extensions`, or this extension's own `chrome-extension://<id>/` pages (§4.26's definition, one port). **Pages the rows need** (all described here, built by S7-helpers, none built by this sheet): (1) `test-fixtures/s12-console.html` + `s12-console.js` (the 0.11 page that logs a sentinel and throws one uncaught error), selected by the query `s12=`: **`con`**: on load logs exactly 10 console messages in order, eight `console.log`, then one `console.warn`, then one `console.error`, each text `s12-con-<i> ` + `SENTINEL` (i = 1…10), then throws one uncaught `Error` with message `s12-con-throw ` + `SENTINEL` from a `setTimeout(…, 0)`; **`rej`**: logs nothing, creates one unhandled rejection of an `Error` with `SENTINEL` and one rejection it handles 200 ms later (revoked); **`big`**: logs one 1 000 000-character ASCII string; **`tick`**: logs `tick <n> ` + `SENTINEL` every 1 000 ms and records each tick's `Date.now()` in `window.__s12.ticks`; each tick also throws one uncaught `Error` (message `s12-exc-<n> ` + `SENTINEL`) from a `setTimeout(…, 0)` and records its `Date.now()` in `window.__s12.excs`; **`frame`**: logs two messages (each `s12-frame-<i> ` + `SENTINEL`) and embeds an `<iframe>` of `http://f.s12site.localhost:8081/s12-console.html?s12=con`; it is loaded as `http://p.s12site.localhost:8081/s12-console.html?s12=frame`, so page and frame share the registrable domain `s12site.localhost` (same site, kept in one process) and the frame's host is the blocked host once the pattern `f.s12site.localhost` is set; **`frameoop`**: the same two messages and an `<iframe>` of `http://127.0.0.1:8081/s12-console.html?s12=con`, the page loaded on `localhost:8081` (a different site, so Chrome's site isolation puts the frame out of process; Stage 0 C5 measured a cross-site frame as an out-of-process frame); **`notea`**: logs one `console.log({e: err})` of an `Error` whose message is `s12-notea ` + `SENTINEL` and one whose message is 3 000 characters starting `s12-notea-long`, from a script file loaded from `http://s12blk.localhost:8081/s12-notea.js` (a blocked host once the pattern is set; the page itself is on `localhost:8081`); **`noteaok`**: logs one `console.log({e: err})` of an `Error` whose message is exactly `s12-noteaok` (no sentinel and nothing else, so the preview stays short: V8's shortening of a preview value "is unmeasured … a live row decides", `S3a.md:66-68`), then one plain `console.log` of `SENTINEL` alone, from a script file loaded from `http://localhost:8081/s12-noteaok.js` (an allowed host); **`nav`**: logs 3 messages, then 400 ms later navigates itself to `…/s12-console.html?s12=nav2`, which logs 2. Every query also sets `window.__s12 = {logged: <n>, thrown: <n>}` (the page's own reporter, read by `evaluate`, counts only) and an effect counter `window.__s12eff` (absent until a row's function sets it). (2) `test-fixtures/e2e-playground.html` (the control tab, as Run 18; **it gains the query `s12=` for S7-helpers to build: at the sheet's HEAD the file has no `s12=` handling, no `#tb` and no `window.__s12` (grep finds 0 matches; its only counter is `#counter`)**: every `s12=` value sets `window.__s12 = {…}`; `as` adds `beat`, a counter advanced by a 100 ms `setInterval`; `g2a` and `g2b` add a text input `#tb` and a button whose clicks are counted in `window.__s12.clicks`; `a1`, `a2`, `a3`, `ag` and `bc` need nothing more) and `test-fixtures/e2e-playground-csp.html` (`script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`, no `unsafe-eval`; its `#eval-probe` line reads `page eval: BLOCKED — EvalError: …` as `docs/e2e-test-plan.md` EVAL-12 writes it). The block list's pattern for R19-CON-3 and R19-CON-NOTEA is `s12blk.localhost`, and for R19-CON-FRAME leg a `f.s12site.localhost` (only `*.localhost` fixture names, as SAVE-BLOCK, §4.18); the pages are loaded **before** the pattern is added and the call runs after it (the read applies the block list afresh, spec §10's S3a note), then the pattern is removed.

**Line forms (part A).** Existing, unchanged and carried from §4.26 by reference: `SAMPLER`, `ROW <id> <STOP|FAIL|NOT DISCRIMINATING|NOT MEASURED|NOT RUN|PASS|RECORD>[ …]` (**exactly one `ROW` per id and slice**), `CHECK`, `ANSWER <row> <code|ok> …`, `LEAKS`, `CALL`, `SCAN <what>: ://=<n> (fixture <n>, other <n>) token=<n> sentinel=<n> profile=<n>`, `RESTORE`, `RULES <n>`, `STATE`, `TOGGLE`, `FOUNDLIST`, `HEAD <12 hex> tree=<clean|dirty>`, `IDLE <row> since-attach-ms=<n>`, `BANNERCTL …`, `RAW …`, the first line `MANIFEST … BUILD … created-url=…` of §4.26's R18-0 and its `S6 commit=ancestor`. **New in part A** (the contract to S7-helpers; each form is compared with this section by key; every value is a count or a fixed code, never a text): (1) `LOADED [rerun=<k> ]consoleMessages=<answered|CODE> entries=<n> console=<c> exception=<x> args=<answered|CODE>`; (2) `ARGS <row> <label> <ok|CODE> value-equal=<yes|no> json-equal=<yes|no>` per table value and `ARGS <row> total table=<n> value-equal=<k> json-equal=<k> via=<sdk|mcp|raw>`; (3) `ARGSREF <label> code=<CODE> exact=<yes|no> data=<none|keys:<k1,k2>> sent=<0|1>` per refusal (`sent` from the actor's own wrapper around `client.call`) and `ARGSREF total refusals=<n> exact=<k> data-none=<d> sent=<w> effects=<e> control=<c>`; (4) `EFF <row> before=<n|absent> after=<n|absent>` (the page's effect counter `window.__s12eff`, read by a plain `evaluate`); (5) `GLOBALS <row> args=<absent|present> fn=<absent|present> new-own-keys=<n>` (the page's own keys before and after the call; names never printed); (6) `STOPCALL <row> answer=<TIMEOUT|CODE|ok> ms=<n> bound-ms=<n> usable=<yes|no> next=<exact|other>`; (7) `OLD <row> leg=<sdk|mcp|ref|file|raw|control> code=<VERSION|ok|CODE> hint=<yes|no> forwarded=<yes|no|n/a>` (`forwarded` is read from the **answer**, never from a counter: on the `raw` leg a call that answers `ok` with a value holding `kind: "function"` was forwarded and run as an expression by the old worker (`yes`); every other leg prints `n/a`; `control` is the call without `args`); (8) `CON <row> <label> entries=<n> console=<c> exception=<x> sentinel=<s> url=<u> stack=<k> maxlen=<m> cut=<k2> uncaught-first=<yes|no> ordered=<yes|no|n/a> newest=<yes|no|n/a> marker=<m|n/a>` (`ordered` = entry timestamps non-decreasing; `newest` = for a `limit` read, the entries equal the last `limit` entries of the unlimited read by the helper's hash, never printed; `marker` = occurrences in the whole reply of the leg's marker strings (R19-CON-NOTEA); a key a leg does not use prints `n/a`; `cut` = entries whose text ends with `…`; `url` = entries whose text holds `://`; `stack` = entries whose text holds `\n    at ` or ` at ` followed by `(`; `sentinel` = entries holding `SENTINEL`; `uncaught-first` = every `exception` entry's text begins `Uncaught`); (9) `CONREF <row> <label> code=<CODE> exact=<yes|no> data=<none|keys:<k1,k2>> same-as=<snapshot|evaluate|none>` (`same-as` = the refusal's code and message equal those of the named control call on the same tab and state); (10) `CONFLOOR disarmed-ticks=<n> in-reply-disarmed=<a> after-ticks=<n> in-reply-after=<b> exc-disarmed=<n> exc-in-reply-disarmed=<c> exc-after=<n> exc-in-reply-after=<d>` (the `exc-` keys count the uncaught errors the `tick` page throws, by their `s12-exc-<n>` index, never their text); (11) `CONMCP <label> lines=<n> form=<f> truncated=<yes|no> entries-via-sdk=<n>` (`form` = lines matching `^\d{4}-\d\d-\d\dT\d\d:\d\d:\d\d\.\d{3}Z (error|warning|info|debug)( uncaught)? `, the formatter's form); (12) `BANNERCON leg=<neg|pos|after> polls=<n> banner=<seen|none> idle-ms=<n>` then `ROW R19-CON-BANNER <PASS|FAIL>`; (13) `DAEMONLOG <row> sentinel=<n> console-text=<n>` is part B's R19-LOGS, not defined here. Tab handles, entry counts and codes only.

a build that reads no frames at all passes the first read of leg a, so **the second read of leg a is the control that frames are read**: `a-nopattern entries=2` makes leg a's drop clause **NOT DISCRIMINATING** (either the frame is not read or Chrome put it out of process; recorded, not a product FAIL, and leg b is then recorded without that control); leg b returning 13 means a build that auto-attaches to child targets, which the spec excludes: FAIL. **Line forms (part B).** Part A's forms and §4.26's are carried. **New in part B** (the contract to S7-helpers; every value a count, a fixed code or a time; never a text): (14) `DEVPROBE method=<helper's name> answer=<DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS|ok|CODE> ms=<n> attached=<yes|no>`, a **row-less** probe (#351 (3)): the helper opens DevTools into a window X the wave opened on a fixture page, then calls `evaluate "1"` on that tab until it answers `DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS` or 15 000 ms pass; `attached=yes` only for `DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS`; (15) `KEEPSEQ <row> calls=<n> gap-ms=<g> answers=<k>/<n> banner=<continuous|gaps:<m>> end-ms=<e> poll-ms=<p>` (`end-ms` = last reply to the first sampler read of `none`; `poll-ms` the sampler's interval); (16) `KEEPEND <row> leg=<ctl|idle|disarm|block|nav-blocked|nav-own|nav-file|close|replace> since-last-call-ms=<n> end-ms=<e> next=<CODE|ok>` (`ctl` = the chooser refusal that drops the session at once, `idle` = no event; both are KEEP-2's references, KEEP-3 compares against them); (17) `CONKEPT first=<n> second=<n> equal=<yes|no> ordered=<yes|no>`; (18) `SWEEPHELD after-reload=<DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS|ok|CODE> devtools-open=<yes|no> reclaimed=<yes|no>`; (19) `KEEPDT <row> leg=<devtools|cancel|closed> next=<CODE|ok|not-measured>`; (20) `OVERLAP <row> leg=<evaluate|chooser> first=<ok|CODE> second=<ok|CODE> exact=<yes|no>`; (21) `CHOOSE <row> <label> <ok|CODE> exact=<yes|no|n/a> files=<n> change=<n> clicks=<n> bytes-equal=<yes|no|n/a> dialog=<n> ms=<n>` (the page's own reporter `window.__s12ch` for `files`, `change`, `clicks`, `bytes-equal`; `dialog` = native dialogs the UIA read counts during the call; `ms` = the call's start to its reply); (22) `DIALOG <row> leg=<a|b|c> before=<n> after-click=<n> after-close=<n> ms=<n> change=<n>` (UIA counts of native file dialogs owned by the wave's window X; `change` = the page's `change` count after the close); (23) `LATE <row> opener-ms=<4900|5100> opened=<yes|no> dialog=<n>`; (24) `HUMAN <row> received=<call-target|clicked-input|none> dialog=<n> files=<n>`; (25) `CONCAP lines=<k> body-chars=<c> marker=<yes|no> first-index=<i> last-index=<j> sentinel=<s>`; (26) `G2 <row> a-reply=<ms> b-reply=<ms> gap-ms=<n> holder-after-reply-ms=<h> a-effect=<n> b=<ok|CODE>` (times from the driver's one clock; `holder-after-reply-ms` = how long a third client's `daemon.status` polls, every 50 ms, kept reading A's client name after A's reply) and `G2TYPE round=<i> sent=40 got=<n> lost=<l> b-calls=<c> b-ok=<k> b-lock-timeout=<t>`; (27) `VERSIONCH <row> leg=<sdk|mcp|raw|control> code=<VERSION|ok|CODE> hint=<yes|no> files=<n> exact=<yes|no>`; (28) `DAEMONLOG <slice> sentinel=<n> console-text=<n> args-text=<n>` (counts in `daemon.log` since the slice's mark of each probe string; the probe strings are fixture constants passed by argument); (29) `SCANTOOL <slice> tool=<name> sentinel=<n> token=<n> profile=<n> shot-line=<n> image-line=<n>` and `SCAN actors: ://=<n> (fixture <n>, other <n>) token=<n> sentinel=<n> profile-other=<n> shot-line=<n> image-line=<n>` (`shot-line` = lines of the MCP failure report that name the saved screenshot, form `Screenshot: …\.chrome-agent-bridge\failures\….png` (#349 (a)); `image-line` = the client's own `[Image: source: …]` lines (#349 (b)); `profile-other` = profile-path matches **outside** those two named lines, `profile-all` = `profile-other` + the matches inside them, printed once as `profile-all=<n>`); (30) `DIALOGS <slice> native=<n> devtools=<n> wave-windows-open=<n>` (read by UIA at a slice's end, all expected 0 except the wave's own windows listed; `devtools` counts DevTools windows **other than** the worker-console capture window, which is saved and closed before the read: Capture segments); (31) `STOP <reason>` with `<reason>` one of `attach-after-disarm`, `attach-on-refused-page`, `kept-session-used`, `dialog-not-opened`, `outside-file-reached-page`, `args-ran-js-off`, `existing-tool-changed`, `dialog-left-open`, `devtools-left-open`, `not-restored`, `text-leak`, `old-build-left-loaded`, `throw-after-state`, and `RETURN <seq> after-stop=<reason> restored=<yes|no|n/a> readback=<equal|n/a>`, printed by the runner when it returns (R7.6); (32) `FIX <what>` for a helper fix made between rows. **The #351 (4) helper fixes S7-helpers makes, as contract** (recipes only, no pass rule moves): (a) when `OPT O2 shown=no` the helper also prints `O2 did not show the saved text in <n> ms` (the row's verdict unchanged); (b) the runner prints the final `ROW <id> <verdict>` of every row that has a console clause (R19-ARGS-*, R19-CON-*) **before** R19-LOGS runs, or resolves the `console-clause pending` lines first, so a stop inside R19-LOGS leaves those rows judged (Run 18 printed `ROW R18-UP-L1` and `ROW R18-SAVE-REFUSE` only after R18-LOGS, #349); (c) the five preflight rows (R19-0's `MANIFEST`/`RULES`/`STATE`/`TOGGLE`/`FOUNDLIST`) print once per slice, so the aggregator prints no `WARN` about `RULES`. Texts the sheet cannot yet quote are named in **Open items**.

**Banner rule and the line forms added by fix round 1.** No attach counter exists and none is built (#351 (2)). The banner sampler is the only live signal, and `recipes/CLAUDE.md` records its limit: "the banner sampler read positive on legs that send no call (R17B-UP-6 `missing`/`relative`), so a positive reading does not show that the call raised the banner" (Run 17b); Run 18 read `seen` on all five R18-UP-6 legs while its control passed (#351 (2)). So **a banner positive on a refused call is a STOP only when both hold: `IDLE >= 20 000 ms` was printed before the call (since the last reply of any call other than `snapshot`, `listTabs` and `daemon.status`; worst case the keeper's 5 s plus Chrome's 14 s, Open item 8), and the slice's R19-BANNER-CTL passed; otherwise it is `NOT MEASURED`, recorded with both values.** The refused calls inside one row are not CDP calls on a correct build, so the row's one idle covers them. A positive that survives both conditions can still come from Chrome or the sampler; the STOP returns for the session agent to judge and is not a product FAIL by itself. **Where no clean window can be made, the row carries no banner STOP:** R19-KEEP-3 and R19-CHOOSE-DISARM (the refused call follows a CDP call of the same row by seconds), and R19-KEEP-1's banner clause is `NOT DISCRIMINATING`; the live proof of "no attach after a disarm" there is the answers and the unit tests, a gap recorded as Open item 5. **New forms** (counts and fixed words only): (33) `CLOSERPROBE method=<helper's name> opened=<yes|no> closed=<yes|no|n/a> ms=<n> native-after=<n>` (R19-CLOSER); (34) `BANNERCALL <row> leg=<label> idle-ms=<n> polls=<n> banner=<seen|none> control=<pass|fail|absent>` (one per banner-judged window, with the sampler of R19-CON-BANNER's form: 500 ms before to 2 500 ms after the reply; `control` = the slice's R19-BANNER-CTL); (35) `CAPSEG <slice> seg=<k> saved=<yes|no> before=<reload|end>` (Capture segments). **Fix round 2: one IDLE, the R19-0 re-runs, and absent keys.** (a) **IDLE has one definition, used by every row and helper: the time since the last reply of any call other than `snapshot`, `listTabs` and `daemon.status`.** The sheet lists no CDP calls and the helper needs no list: this superset counts every call that can attach. The source is the phase doc's § 0.6: "the first CDP call attaches, later calls reuse it" and "`withDebugger` and every caller (`extension/src/debugger.ts`) go through it". Every "since the last CDP call", "since the control" and "since the last reply of any CDP call" below means this IDLE. (b) **An R19-0 re-run prints only `LOADED rerun=<k> …` (k = 1, 2, …): no `ROW R19-0` line and no preflight lines**, so each id prints exactly one `ROW` per slice; form (1)'s `LOADED` line carries the optional `rerun=<k>` key first. (c) **A key left out of an expected `CON` line means "not judged"**: the keys `ordered`, `newest` and `marker` are judged only on the rows and legs that write them.

**Part B's fixture pages and ports** (described for S7-helpers, none built here; same one server on 8081, §4.27's ports line holds): (4) `test-fixtures/s12-chooser.html` + `s12-chooser.js`, query `s12=`: **`c1`**: a visible `<button id="open">` whose click calls a hidden `<input id="in" type="file">`'s `click()`, plus a visible `<input id="vis" type="file">` (for CHOOSE-4's human-style click); **`label`**: a `<label id="lab" for="in2">` and `<input id="in2" type="file" multiple>`, and the input as a target; **`none`**: a button whose click does nothing, and a visible `<input id="vis" type="file">`; **`picker`**: a button that calls `showOpenFilePicker()`; **`single`**: a button opening a single-file input, and a visible `<input id="vis" type="file">`; **`dir`**: a button opening a `webkitdirectory` input; **`dis`**: a button opening a `disabled` input; **`late49`** and **`late51`**: a button whose click arms a `setTimeout` calling the hidden input's `click()` after 4 900 ms and 5 100 ms (the page records `late.opened=<yes|no|error>` itself); **`human`**: a no-op button and a second visible `<input id="second" type="file">`; **`frame`**: a same-origin `<iframe src="s12-chooser-frame.html">` holding a button and a hidden input. Every query sets `window.__s12ch = {clicks, change, files, bytes, bytesEqual, late}` (the page counts its own `click` and `change` events, reads the received file's size and compares a page-computed digest with the one the helper passed in the URL hash `#sum=<hex>`; names and digests never leave the page). (5) `test-fixtures/s12-chooser-frame.html` (the iframe's document). (6) `test-fixtures/s12-console.html` gains the queries **`cap`** (200 messages `console.log("s12-cap-<i>:" padded with x to 2000 characters, SENTINEL included in the pad's first 40 characters)`, i = 1…200). KEEP rows use `s12-console.html?s12=con` (calls: `evaluate`, `consoleMessages`, a trusted `click` on the page's own `<body>`, a `screenshot` with `mode: "cdp"`, an `uploadFiles` on `s11-upload.html`) and `s12-chooser.html?s12=single` for KEEP-2's control; G2 rows use `e2e-playground.html?s12=g2a` (its text input `#tb`, its `click` counter read by `evaluate`) and `e2e-playground.html?s12=g2b` in a second window. The own-page, `file:` and blocked-host navigation targets of KEEP-3 are part A's (`chrome-extension://<id>/options.html`, the wave's `file:` fixture, `s12blk.localhost:8081`). No other port; each leg closes its own tab.

A call answering differently is the STOP `existing-tool-changed`. **The banner clause is `NOT DISCRIMINATING`, and the row prints `ROW R19-KEEP-1 PASS banner-clause=NOT DISCRIMINATING`: so `PASS` stands for the answers unchanged only, never for "one attach for N calls".** A banner gap (`banner=gaps:<m>`) between calls 3 s apart does fail the row (a kept session cannot drop there); `continuous` proves nothing about keeping. **What the row cannot show, written now:** how many attaches happened (no counter exists and none is built, #351 (2)); one attach for N calls is proved by S4a/S4b's fake-counted unit tests, and this row proves their live consequence only: answers unchanged and a banner that does not drop between calls 3 s apart. A per-call build also passes the banner clause when Chrome's linger is over 3 s (Stage 0 measured ~5.1 s), so the row **fails a build that does not keep only if its linger is short**; KEEP-2's control is the discriminating row. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-keep-1` → `Invoke-S11Sampler`. Source: 0.11 seed KEEP-1; `S4b.md` §7 (KEEP-1: one attach across sequential CDP tools, steady banner, answers as today); phase doc 0.6 |
| R19-KEEP-2 ★ (0.11 KEEP-2; idle detach and the banner's end time, with a per-call control) | B; JS on, armed | On the `s12=con` tab: (idle leg) one `evaluate "1"`, then no call, the sampler to 25 000 ms after the reply; **three times**, each after `idle ≥ 20 000`; (ctl leg) on `…/s12-chooser.html?s12=single` three times: `tab.upload(<single-input button>, [<ROOT>\s11-1k.bin, <ROOT>\s11-sent.bin], {chooser: true})`, which refuses after the interception is on (the single input takes one file) and so **ends the use with an error and drops the session at once** (spec §10 S5a note: "Any refusal or error after the interception was asked for also ends the use with an error, so the keeper detaches the session (#347)"); the sampler to 25 000 ms after the reply, each after `idle ≥ 20 000` | `KEEPEND R19-KEEP-2 leg=idle since-last-call-ms=0 end-ms=<e1> next=ok` ×3 and `KEEPEND R19-KEEP-2 leg=ctl since-last-call-ms=0 end-ms=<c1> next=BAD_PARAMS` ×3 (the ctl refusal is `SINGLE_FILE_INPUT_MESSAGE`: `the file input takes one file, and more than one path was given`, `extension/src/content/upload-target.ts:31`) | **Median(idle `end-ms`) − median(ctl `end-ms`) ≥ 3 500 ms** (the idle bound is 5 000 ms, phase doc 0.6; 1 500 ms of margin), **and the spread of the three ctl readings ≤ 3 000 ms**, else the clause is NOT MEASURED (the control has no resolution: the old linger was 3–14 s, SAFE-4 (b)); every `end-ms` ≤ 25 000 (the banner ends). The three values and both medians are recorded (V3-4 and V4-1 of `S4b.md` §5 wait on them). **Discrimination:** a per-call build detaches at the reply on both legs, so idle − ctl ≈ 0 and fails; a build that never detaches shows no `none` by 25 000 and fails; the ctl leg is the same CDP path with an immediate detach, so the same linger enters both medians. The row writes down what S4b did not: the sampler measures the banner, not the session, so a banner-side change by Chrome would move both legs alike and cancel. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-keep-2`. Source: 0.11 seed KEEP-2; `S4b.md` §7 (KEEP-2); phase doc 0.6; spec §10 S5a note (the refusal that drops the session) |
| R19-KEEP-3 ★ (0.11 KEEP-3; a disarm and a page that turns blocked, own or `file:` end the session; the gate re-check) | B; **disarmed** then on; block list gains `s12blk.localhost` then restored | Each leg on a tab the wave opened, after `idle ≥ 20 000`: a CDP call (`evaluate "1"`) then **1 000 ms later** the event, then one more call 1 000 ms after it: (disarm) the toolbar button pressed (off) then on after the call; (block) the pattern `s12blk.localhost` added in the wave's options window for a tab on that host loaded before it, then removed; (nav-blocked) the tab navigated to `s12blk.localhost:8081` with the pattern set; (nav-own) the tab navigated to `chrome-extension://<id>/options.html`; (nav-file) navigated to the wave's `file:` fixture (only with `TOGGLE reading on`, else `NOT RUN — waits for G11-3`); (close) the tab closed by `tab.close()`; (replace) **not triggered** (no deterministic way to cause a Memory-Saver replace is sourced): `NOT RUN`. After each leg the sampler runs to 25 000 ms | `KEEPEND R19-KEEP-3 leg=disarm since-last-call-ms=1000 end-ms=<e> next=DISARMED`; `leg=block … next=DOMAIN_BLOCKED`; `leg=nav-blocked … DOMAIN_BLOCKED`; `leg=nav-own … DOMAIN_BLOCKED`; `leg=nav-file … DOMAIN_BLOCKED`; `leg=close … next=TAB_GONE` (the code only: the answer carries `data {lastUrl, lastTitle}`, `daemon/src/intercept.ts:601` at HEAD, never printed, and its message is not compared); each text exact as part A's rows quote it (`BLOCKED_TAB_MESSAGE`, `OWN_PAGE_MESSAGE`, `FILE_PAGE_MESSAGE`, K-disarmed with `data {"scope":"all"}`) | **The next call after the event answers the refusal exactly** (a kept session never runs a call the gate would refuse: phase doc 0.6, "the gate still runs per call"); **an `ok` or any other reply is the STOP `kept-session-used`**. **The session ended at the event, not at the idle bound:** `end-ms(leg)` measured from the last call, within **2 000 ms of KEEP-2's ctl median** and **at least 2 500 ms below KEEP-2's idle median** (the event came 1 s after the call, the idle path ends 5 s after it), judged only if KEEP-2's clause was measured, else this clause is NOT MEASURED. **No banner STOP in this row:** the refused call comes 2 s after a CDP call of the same row, and Chrome's banner lingers about 5.1 s after a detach (Stage 0), so a positive on the refused window is the linger of the session the event just ended (`recipes/CLAUDE.md`: a positive reading "does not show that the call raised the banner"); the `end-ms` clauses above already measure the banner's end. That no attach follows a disarm or a refused page is proved live by the answers (`kept-session-used`) and by the unit tests (the gate at receipt and again before the attach); `attach-after-disarm` and `attach-on-refused-page` are **NOT DISCRIMINATING in this row** (Open item 5). **Discrimination:** a build that keeps the session across the disarm ends at the idle bound, so `end-ms` equals the idle median (fails the 2 500 ms margin) and a build that skips the re-check answers `ok` (STOP). | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-keep-3` (`Set-S9BlockList`, the toolbar press). Source: 0.11 seed KEEP-3; `S4b.md` §7 (KEEP-3); phase doc 0.6 (detach on); decision #352 |

The human's Cancel and DevTools always win (phase doc 0.6). X closed at the end (`devtools-left-open` is a STOP). **Discrimination:** a build whose kept session ignores `onDetach` answers an error other than K-devt or keeps answering K-devt after DevTools is gone. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-keep-4`. | R19-G2-1 ★ (G6.8; a trusted input holds the lock to a 500 ms settle: another client's screenshot waits it out) | C; two clients A and B, one fixture tab each, two windows; JS on, armed | A: a **raw** `client.call("click", {tab, ref, trusted: true})` on `e2e-playground.html?s12=g2a` (no SDK `focusFirst`, so no earlier `focusTab` frees the lock before the daemon's own focus); B: a default `screenshot` (the visibility-lock mode) of its tab, started 100 ms after A's send; a third client C polls `daemon.status` every 50 ms; the page's click counter read | `G2 R19-G2-1 a-reply=<ms> b-reply=<ms> gap-ms=<b-reply − a-reply> holder-after-reply-ms=<h≥450> a-effect=1 b=ok` | A's click `ok` first; **`b-reply − a-reply` ≥ 450 ms** (S6-G2-builder's bound for the 500 ms hold, "bound 450 ms, to allow for clock reading"); `holder-after-reply-ms` ≥ 450 (C reads A's client name from `daemon.status.lock.holder` during the hold); `a-effect=1` (T_A's counter +1); B answers `ok`, no `LOCK_TIMEOUT`. **Discrimination:** a build with no hold releases at A's reply, so B's reply follows within a screenshot's own time and `holder-after-reply-ms` is about 0; the SDK-default-policy variant (A with `focusFirst`) is run second and **only recorded** (its own `focusTab` frees the lock first, #354: "no deadlock … the cost one round trip and up to 500 ms"). | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-g2-1`. Source: `S6-G2.md` §7; `S6-G2-builder.md` §7 (R19-G2-1 draft); spec §10 S6-G2 note; decision #354 |
| R19-G2-2 ★ (G6.8; a typed string complete under a competing capture) | C; as R19-G2-1 | A: raw `client.call("type", {tab, ref, text: <40-character fixture string>, trusted: true})` into `#tb`; B loops default screenshots of its tab (at least 3, the first within 50 ms of A's send); **five rounds**, the input cleared between rounds | `G2TYPE round=<i> sent=40 got=40 lost=0 b-calls=<c≥3> b-ok=<c> b-lock-timeout=0` ×5 | A's `type` answers `ok` and the input value (read by `evaluate`, counted) equals the string in **5 of 5 rounds**, every B screenshot `ok`, none `LOCK_TIMEOUT` under a 30 s timeout. **The clause S6-G2 said cannot be observed live, quoted:** "No B screenshot is forwarded between A's daemon `focusTab` and A's input reply" (`S6-G2-builder.md` §7, line 128; the verifier's MINOR-4: "No log line or status field shows when the daemon forwards. A live runner sees only client-side send and reply times", `S6-G2-verifier.md`). **How the row stands without it, in client-observable terms (the verifier's fix):** (i) no B screenshot sent after A's send replies before A's reply + 450 ms (`gap-ms` of every B call is printed; a B call already holding the lock before A's send is not in the set); (ii) `daemon.status.lock.holder` reads A during the hold (R19-G2-1's reading); (iii) the effect, A's text complete. The row **cannot prove** that no capture came between the focus and the input in a round where the text arrived intact; it fails a build that lets one in only when the capture steals focus and a character is lost (`lost>0`), and five rounds raise that chance. Written plainly: a pass is evidence of no harm, not of the absence of an interleaving. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-g2-2`. Source: `S6-G2.md` §7 and `S6-G2-builder.md` §7 (G6.8 drafts); `S6-G2-verifier.md` MINOR-4; decision #354 |

The mechanism is the hello's `features` carrying `"uploadChooser"` (decision #355). **Discrimination:** a build that detects by the answer or ignores `chooser` uploads into the input (`files=1`: the misleading success #355 prevents). After the row: the current build rebuilt, reloaded and **R19-0 re-run with `LOADED … args=answered`, else STOP `old-build-left-loaded`**. If `63c771c` cannot be loaded within one re-run (exit 2 twice) the row is `NOT RUN — old build not loadable`. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-version` (a scratch-worktree swap like R19-ARGS-OLD's). Source: decision #355; spec §10 S5c note (`git show HEAD:docs/specs/2026-10-21-daemon-sdk-multi-actor-design.md`: "The text names no path"; an `uploadFiles` without `chooser` "is forwarded to a featureless extension as before"); S5b.md F3 |
| R19-LOGS ★ (R7.5, per slice; a NEW row, never an edit of R18-LOGS; #349) | A, B, C, D, E (end of each, after R19-RESTORE) | Over **this slice's own** console capture (`console-<slice>.txt` for a slice with no extension reload; **`console-<slice>-<k>.txt` segments for B, D and E, see Capture segments**; the control line `safety: disarmed by the user` written by R19-RESTORE's press, in the last segment), `daemon.log` since the slice's mark, and each actor's tool **results** (never its inputs), as §4.26's R18-LOGS; **the actors scan reads product text only: the MCP failure report's screenshot line and the client's image line are counted separately and named** (#349); the console/daemon scans also carry what part A deferred here: **S3a check 9** ("A sentinel in the console text appears in the reply only. It is absent from the worker console and `daemon.log`") and **S3b's CON-LOGS** ("a sentinel logged by the fixture is counted 0 in `daemon.log` and the worker console"), and S2b's `args` sentinel (**the args sentinel** is the fixture constant of R19-ARGS-1's fifth table value, a value distinct from `SENTINEL`, defined by S7-helpers and passed to the scanners by argument as `SENTINEL` is) | Per slice: `SCAN console: ://=<n> (fixture <n>, other 0) token=0 sentinel=0 profile=0`; `SCAN daemon: … sentinel=0 …`; `DAEMONLOG <slice> sentinel=0 console-text=0 args-text=0`; `SCAN actors: ://=<n> (fixture <n>, other 0) token=0 sentinel=<n> profile-other=0 shot-line=<a> image-line=<b>` with `profile-all=<a+b+…>` and one `SCANTOOL` per tool; `CONSOLE <slice> segments=<k> capture=<file(s)> texts=<n> control-disarmed=<n≥1 in the last segment>` | **Token, sentinel (fixture constants `SENTINEL`, the args sentinel, passed by argument), profile path and every non-fixture `scheme://host` are 0 in the console and daemon streams** (a non-zero count is a STOP, `text-leak`); `DAEMONLOG` probes 0 (`SENTINEL`, `s12-con-`, `s12-notea`, the args sentinel). **In the actors' results the sentinel is expected, by design, only in the tools whose reply carries it** (`browser_console_messages`, and `browser_evaluate`'s own echoed value): `SCANTOOL` shows `sentinel>0` for those tools only and **0 for every other tool** (a refusal line, a snapshot, an upload answer); `token=0` everywhere; **`profile-other=0`** (matches outside the two named lines); `shot-line` and `image-line` are printed as the two findings of #349 (a) and (b), expected only in a failure-report-bearing result (`shot-line`) and an image item (`image-line`), **never counted against the row** but each must match its own form, else it is counted in `profile-other`. The capture holds the control line (`control-disarmed` ≥ 1, in the last segment), else NOT MEASURED for that slice; a segment not saved before a reload (`CAPSEG … saved=no`) also makes it NOT MEASURED; a token that cannot be found is NOT MEASURED. **Discrimination:** Run 18's slice A read `profile=2` from exactly these two lines and could not tell a product leak from them; a leak in a refusal text, a snapshot or an upload answer lands in `profile-other` and fails the row, and a sentinel in `daemon.log` or the worker console (a logging regression) is a STOP. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-logs` → `S9 scan-log`, `S9 scan-file`, `S11 scan`, the actors scanner split by tool. Source: §4.26 R18-RESTORE; phase doc R7.4; STOP rules |

**Slices and order (#271).** One server (8081) and the Prologue as §4.26 (ledger: preflight, server, files `<ROOT>`, `<OUTSIDE>`, `s11-1k.bin`, `s11-sent.bin`, `s11-out.bin`, the sentinel folder, a `file:` fixture outside the repo, **the slice's own console capture started before R19-0**, `npm run build` from a clean tree at a committed HEAD, reload by `recipes/reload-extension.ps1`). **R19-0 is the first row of each slice and proves the loaded build before any state change (R7.2);** **R19-RESTORE then R19-LOGS end every slice**; a row that changes the safety state restores it before the next row. Runner attempts: at most three per slice (phase doc § S7 as S1; §7's fix rounds apply).
- **liveA19 (args and console; JS on, armed; the block list and JS switch are edited here):** R19-0; **R19-BANNER-CTL**; R19-ARGS-1, R19-ARGS-2, R19-ARGS-GLOBAL, R19-ARGS-CSP, R19-ARGS-STOP; R19-CON-1, R19-CON-2, R19-CON-REJ, R19-CON-BIG, R19-CON-NAV, R19-CON-MCP, R19-CON-CAP, then, each restored before the next: **R19-CON-BANNER** (its own negative leg first, 20 s idle), R19-ARGS-3 (JS off), R19-CON-JSOFF (JS off), R19-CON-3, R19-CON-FRAME, R19-CON-NOTEA (block list gains `s12blk.localhost`), R19-CON-4 and R19-CON-FLOOR (toolbar disarm), R19-CON-OWN, R19-CON-FILE (only with `TOGGLE reading on`); R19-RESTORE; R19-LOGS. No DevTools window opens in this slice other than the worker-console capture window, the wave's one named exception (Capture segments).
- **liveB19 (the kept session, the carried banner rows, DevTools; JS on, armed; ROOT added):** R19-0; **the row-less `DEVPROBE` (window X opened, DevTools opened by the helper's method, `evaluate` until K-devt or 15 s, X closed; its line printed; an `attached=no` result makes R19-UP-DEVT, R19-KEEP-4 and R19-SWEEP-HELD `NOT MEASURED — DevTools was not attached`)**; **the row-less `CLOSERPROBE` (R19-CLOSER) right after it, before any row: R19-KEEP-2's ctl leg is the slice's one `chooser: true` call and can raise a native dialog if the interception fails**; **R19-UP-OPT first among the rows, after the investigator's report** (the investigator is a read-only leaf run before this slice, no state change; #351 (1)); R19-BANNER-CTL; R19-UP-6; R19-F-RAW, -2, -DIR; R19-KEEP-1; R19-KEEP-2; R19-KEEP-3 (disarm, block list; each restored before the next); R19-CON-KEPT; R19-KEEP-OVERLAP leg a; **then the DevTools rows, after every non-DevTools row of the slice:** R19-UP-DEVT, R19-KEEP-4, R19-DEVT-NOFLAG, and **last R19-SWEEP-HELD** (it reloads the extension; the capture is saved before the reload and reopened after it, before the re-run, Capture segments; R19-0 is re-run after it as a `LOADED rerun=<k> …` line only); R19-RESTORE; R19-LOGS.
- **liveC19 (the file chooser and G2; JS on, armed; ROOT added; window X in front for CHOOSE-4):** R19-0; **R19-BANNER-CTL; the row-less `CLOSERPROBE` (R19-CLOSER), before every row that can raise a native dialog**; **R19-CHOOSE-1, R19-CHOOSE-LABEL, R19-CHOOSE-REFUSE first** (S5a V6); R19-CHOOSE-2, R19-CHOOSE-3, R19-CHOOSE-MCP (legs a–c, e), R19-CHOOSE-KEPT, R19-CHOOSE-FRAME, R19-CHOOSE-HUMAN, R19-CHOOSE-LATE, R19-KEEP-OVERLAP leg b; **R19-CHOOSE-4** (three native dialogs, each closed before the next leg); R19-CHOOSE-DISARM (toolbar), R19-CHOOSE-JSOFF and R19-CHOOSE-MCP leg d (JS off, restored); R19-G2-1, R19-G2-2 (two clients); R19-RESTORE; R19-LOGS (the dialogs' closure read by `DIALOGS`).
- **liveD19 (old build, args):** **R19-0 first, in full, on the current build** (its `LOADED … args=answered` and R18-0's state lines are the slice's reference for R19-RESTORE, R7.4); the swap to the pre-S2a build (capture segment saved before, reopened after); the old-build proof (`LOADED consoleMessages=UNKNOWN_METHOD …`, a line of its own, not R19-0); **R19-ARGS-OLD** (part A: the wave's last row in its slice); the current build reloaded (segment saved before, reopened after, before the re-run), **R19-0 re-run (a `LOADED rerun=<k> …` line only)**; R19-RESTORE; R19-LOGS. **liveE19 (old build, chooser):** **R19-0 first, in full, on the current build**; `63c771c` built and loaded (segments as in liveD19), **R19-VERSION**, the current build reloaded, **R19-0 re-run (a `LOADED rerun=<k> …` line only)**; R19-RESTORE; R19-LOGS.
- **Where the carried and the control rows sit:** each of liveA19, liveB19 and liveC19 holds one R19-BANNER-CTL at 20 000 ms idle (the Banner rule); R19-CON-BANNER's own negative and after legs, in liveA19, are 20 000 ms too (Open item 8); the **UP-OPT investigator is not a row** and runs once before liveB19; **the DevTools probe is the only DevTools step before liveB19's DevTools rows**. No G1 row (G1 waits for G12-2, #337).
- **STOP lines matched to a return (R7.6):** every `STOP <reason>` line (31) is followed by a `RETURN <seq> after-stop=<reason> restored=<yes|no|n/a> readback=<equal|n/a>` before the next row; the reasons and where they arise: `attach-after-disarm` (R19-CON-4 only, under the Banner rule; R19-KEEP-3 and R19-CHOOSE-DISARM carry none); `attach-on-refused-page` (R19-CON-3, -OWN, -FILE, -JSOFF, R19-ARGS-3, R19-CHOOSE-2, -JSOFF, under the Banner rule; not R19-KEEP-3); `kept-session-used` (R19-KEEP-3: an `ok` where the gate refuses); `dialog-not-opened` (R19-CHOOSE-4, -DISARM: no dialog on the human-style click; it is the one reason for "the interception is still on", and no other row prints a separate reason for it); `outside-file-reached-page` (R19-CHOOSE-2, -HUMAN, any upload row); `args-ran-js-off` (R19-ARGS-3); `existing-tool-changed` (R19-ARGS-1's controls, R19-CON-MCP, R19-CON-KEPT, R19-KEEP-1, R19-CHOOSE-JSOFF, R19-CHOOSE-MCP leg e); `dialog-left-open` (R19-CLOSER, R19-CHOOSE-*, R19-RESTORE) and `devtools-left-open` (R19-UP-DEVT, R19-KEEP-4, R19-SWEEP-HELD, R19-RESTORE); `not-restored` (R19-RESTORE); `text-leak` (R19-LOGS); `old-build-left-loaded` (R19-ARGS-OLD, R19-VERSION, R19-RESTORE); `throw-after-state` (any slice). R7.6 passes when every `STOP` has exactly one `RETURN` after it, in order, and the final ledger of each slice holds no unmatched line either way.

**Capture segments (R7.5 across reloads; fix round 1).** The slice's console capture is a DevTools window on the extension's service worker (`recipes/s11-r16.ps1`, block `console`: `CONSOLE the worker console window is open`), and an extension reload replaces that worker, so the window and what it showed are lost (Run 17b lost a capture that way, #318). Slices B (R19-SWEEP-HELD's reload), D and E (two reloads each) therefore keep the capture in **segments**: before every reload the helper saves the capture to the slice's next file (`console-<slice>-<k>.txt`, k = 1, 2, …; the paths all differ and a path that already holds text is a STOP, as §4.26's guard), prints `CAPSEG <slice> seg=<k> saved=<yes|no> before=<reload|end>` and closes the capture window; after the reload and **before** R19-0's re-run it reopens a worker console (the helper of the `console` block), so the re-run's worker output is captured, as the Prologue starts the capture before R19-0. R19-LOGS scans **all segments of the slice as one stream** (counts summed), and the control line (R19-RESTORE's press) must be in the **last** segment. A segment not saved before a reload (`saved=no`) makes R19-LOGS NOT MEASURED for the slice. Slices A and C reload nothing and keep one segment (`console-liveA19.txt`, `console-liveC19.txt`). **The capture window is the wave's one named exception to every "no DevTools" sentence** (as §4.26's capture): it is not a window X, `DIALOGS … devtools=<n>` counts DevTools windows other than it, and R19-RESTORE saves the last segment and closes it before that read, so `devtools=0` holds with no capture window left; a capture window still open at the slice's end is the STOP `devtools-left-open`, as any DevTools window.

**Open items, recorded before the run (not failures; the session agent rules, never a builder):** From part A: (1) **R19-ARGS-OLD needs a pre-S2a build** (`3ab9207`, a scratch worktree with its own `npm ci`, never a shared `node_modules`); `NOT RUN` if it cannot be loaded. (2) **Bounds part A chose without a source:** `STOPCALL ms` 1 500–3 500; ±500 ms around the toolbar press in R19-CON-FLOOR; the page's 8/1/1 level mix. (3) **Class of `consoleMessages`:** phase doc 0.5 says class `tab`; #334 and the spec's S3a note class it `evaluate` (JavaScript off refuses it); the sheet follows #334. (4) **Whether Chrome put a frame out of process is not observable by an SDK actor** (`Target.getTargets` is refused through `chrome.debugger`, Stage 0 C5); R19-CON-FRAME builds the two cases by the site rule (a same-site pair on `*.s12site.localhost`, a cross-site pair `localhost` / `127.0.0.1`) and its two legs prove it together; `a-nopattern entries=2` makes leg a's drop clause NOT DISCRIMINATING. (5) **No attach counter exists and none is built (#351 (2))**; fix round 1 struck every "counted by the keeper" clause and put the **Banner rule** in its place (20 s idle plus a per-slice control). **Not discriminating live, by design:** `attach-after-disarm` and `attach-on-refused-page` in R19-KEEP-3, `attach-after-disarm` in R19-CHOOSE-DISARM, and R19-KEEP-1's banner clause; their proof is the answers (`kept-session-used`) and the unit tests (the gate at receipt and again before the attach). Whether that gap is acceptable or the instrument (G12-2) is wanted is the session agent's to rule; the step agent raises it, unconditionally, under agent-rules §3 item 9 (a check that cannot be made discriminating) and reports it upward. From part B: (6) *Closed by fix round 1:* S5c's texts are committed (`11a1210`, `d38b590`) and quoted into R19-VERSION. (7) **The pre-S5a build is `63c771c`** (S4b, the last commit before S5a's spec note `8eab22c`; named in decision #352); S5c's own commit does not matter to it. (8) **Idle bounds:** part A's R19-CON-BANNER waited 15 s in the first draft (measured banner 5.1 s after a detach plus the 5 s keeper bound is about 10 s), while §4.26's older reading was a 3–14 s linger, so 5 + 14 = 19 s is the worst case; fix round 1 raised CON-BANNER's negative and after legs, and every banner control, to 20 000 ms; a `seen` on CON-BANNER's negative leg is NOT MEASURED by its own rule, and on its after leg at 20 s a FAIL (past the worst case). (9) **Bounds part B wrote without a source:** CHOOSE-3 `ms` 4 900–8 000; CHOOSE-DISARM `ms` ≤ 7 000; KEEP-2's 3 500 ms margin and 3 000 ms spread; KEEP-3's 2 000 and 2 500 ms margins; the sampler `poll-ms` ≤ 250; the 50 ms status poll and the 100 ms offset of G2 (the 450 ms bound is S6-G2's). (10) **The sampler measures the banner, not the session**, so R19-KEEP-1 cannot count attaches and R19-KEEP-2 is a differential against a control; if KEEP-2's control has no resolution (spread over 3 000 ms), KEEP-2 and KEEP-3's timing clauses are NOT MEASURED and the kept session is proved only by its tests. (11) **The DevTools opening method is unproven** (F12 attached nothing twice; no sourced method); the `DEVPROBE` decides, and every DevTools row is NOT MEASURED without it. **The infobar's Cancel button by UIA** has no sourced method (R19-KEEP-4's cancel leg is optional). (12) **Closing Chrome's native file dialog is unproven** (#251): S7-helpers' closer (owner window X, close pattern, Cancel, Escape to that window only) needs its own unit tests, and **the closer is exercised first by the row-less `CLOSERPROBE` (R19-CLOSER) on a dialog the helper opens itself, under a 5 000 ms watchdog, before the first chooser call of liveB19 (R19-KEEP-2's ctl leg) and of liveC19 (R19-CHOOSE-1 onward can raise a dialog if the interception fails); a failing closer is the returning STOP `dialog-left-open`.** (13) **Memory-Saver replace** (R19-KEEP-3's `replace` leg) has no sourced trigger: `NOT RUN`. (14) **The UP-OPT investigator's report** does not exist yet; R19-UP-OPT waits for it. (15) **The `Screenshot:` and `[Image: source:` forms** in R19-LOGS come from #349's prose (`decisions.md` #349), not from a committed test; S7-helpers pins them against the committed failure-report test before the run. (16) The formatter cap's direction (oldest kept) is for the user's ruling (#355); the row records it either way. (17) **The old-build rows** (ARGS-OLD, VERSION) each need two reloads and a rebuilt `dist/` (gitignored); the order puts them last so a failed swap loses no other row.

*Note 2026-10-28, before any Run 19 observation (session agent; decisions.md #358):* forms (15) `KEEPSEQ` and (16) `KEEPEND`
accept `end-ms=never` (the banner did not go off within the sampler's window), which their rows judge as "no end seen"
(a FAIL of an end-time clause, never a PASS). The other wording gaps the helpers found (`…-steps/S7-helpers-a.md` §6,
`S7-helpers-b.md` §6: `cut=` missing from three expected `CON` lines, R19-CON-NOTEA's key order, `late.opened` vs the
page key `late`, R19-CON-BANNER's NOT MEASURED, `DAEMONLOG` defined as (13) and (28)) are read as those reports state,
fixed here before the run; no pass rule changes.

*Note 2026-10-28, before any Run 19 observation (session agent; decisions.md #361):* R19-CON-FLOOR's waits are
raised from 3 s (disarmed) and 2.5 s (after) to **5 s** and **3.5 s**: the page ticks once a second and the row's
±500 ms margins leave only two judged ticks in 3 s against its `≥ 3` (S7-drivers-A N1, from the fixture's
`setInterval(…, 1000)`, not from a run); the thresholds and every clause are unchanged. R19-CON-4's step text calls the
worker's `safety: disarmed by the user` log line a title: it is the log line (NIT-A). The headless MCP actor of
ARGS-1, ARGS-3, CON-MCP and CON-CAP is a scripted stdio MCP client of `mcp/dist/index.js` (the same server and tool
text, no model); its `browser_list_tabs`/`browser_status` results are kept in `actors-<slice>.jsonl` as counts and fixed
words only (never a title or URL of the user's tabs).

counts, CHOOSE-1's page per call, one CLOSERPROBE) are read as that report states.

*Note 2026-10-29, before any Run 19 observation (session agent; decisions.md #365):* **liveE19: ROOT added** inside
the R19-VERSION block, because its control upload needs it and liveE19's order has no root-add. R19-RESTORE restores the
Upload folders and reads them back by hash, as in every slice. An old build that cannot be loaded makes its row
`NOT RUN — old build not loadable`. That is detected from the reload block's `exit 2 (attempt 2)` record line, and the
record then shows the guard's `STOP:` line, the `NOT RUN` row and no `RETURN`, by design. In R19-ARGS-OLD's raw leg,
"nothing forwarded" cannot be told apart live from a daemon that forwards the call and rewrites the answer to `VERSION`.
That clause is proved by `mcp/test/s12-args-cross.test.ts`, and live it is not discriminating. The pass rule is unchanged.

*Note 2026-10-29, **after** liveA19 attempt 1's observation (session agent; decisions.md #366; stated as a correction,
not as a pre-run note):* R19-ARGS-1's control-2 expected value `kind: "function"` for the raw no-`args` call
`evaluate "function f() {}"` was copied from `mcp/test/s12-args-cross.test.ts`, which runs against a fake extension. In
a real page that source is a function declaration, not an expression. Attempt 1 answered `ok` with
`kind: "undefined"`, and its STOP `existing-tool-changed` fired on the wrong constant (`…-steps/S7-live-liveA19.md`).
The control's purpose is unchanged: "a call without `args` answers as before". So **"as before" is now the old build's own
answer, measured**:
- **In liveA19, control-2 must answer `ok`.** A non-`ok` answer is still the STOP. Its `kind` is recorded as a
  `CHECK R19-ARGS-1 control-2 kind=<k>` line.
- **In liveD19, R19-ARGS-OLD's raw no-`args` twin sends the same `function f() {}` call to the pre-S2a build.** It
  records `kind=<k>` and is the reference.
- **S7-record judges control-2:** the same `kind` on both builds is PASS; a different one is FAIL
  `existing-tool-changed` (a STOP can no longer fire there). If ARGS-OLD is NOT RUN, control-2 is NOT MEASURED.

R19-ARGS-OLD's clause "its raw twin `ok` with `kind: "function"`" is read the same way: `ok`, kind recorded. Its raw
`args` leg's `(a) => a` is an arrow expression, so its `kind: "function"` reasoning stands.

```
ACT-1 [P] ACT-2 [P]
(other rows not re-run this pass — see Run 1, 2026-06-04, in §0 of docs/progress-and-roadmap.md)
```

Scope: PERC-5…9 (never run live before) plus a regression smoke over PERC-1…4 / ACT-1…2 to confirm
the Phase C snapshot rewrite didn't regress the earlier pass. **All 13 cases passed on the first
attempt; no code changes were needed.** Service-worker console clean (no errors) for the whole run.

Evidence per case:

- **PERC-1/2** — first snapshot: `textbox "Email address" [ref=e1]`, `textbox "Password" [ref=e2]`
  (via `aria-labelledby`), `textbox "Search query" [ref=e3]` (via placeholder),
  `combobox "Favorite fruit" [ref=e4]`, `button "Sign in" [ref=e5]`, `link "Jump to bottom" [ref=e9]`.
- **PERC-3/4** — viewport PNG renders; `fullPage:true` returns a taller PNG containing `BOTTOM MARKER`.
  The debugging banner appeared for the full-page capture and cleared afterwards.
- **ACT-1/2** — `browser_type e1 "a@b.com"` → `status: email = a@b.com`;
  `browser_click e6` → snapshot `Click counter: 1`, screenshot `status: counter = 1`.
- **PERC-5 ★** — `button "Shadow button" [ref=e10]` listed; `Sealed button (must not be listed)` absent
  from the snapshot even though the screenshot shows it *rendered* (so the exclusion is the closed-root
  rule, not a missing element). Click → `status: shadow button clicked`. The fixture attaches that
  listener to the button *inside* the open root and gives the host `<div>` none, so the RefMap held the
  shadow element, not the host.
- **PERC-6 ★** — `button "Iframe button" [ref=e11]` present on the **first** snapshot (no frame-population
  timing issue). Click → `status: iframe button clicked (isTrusted = false)`, frame `<p>`:
  `iframe: synthetic click`.
- **PERC-7** — `browser_click {ref:e11, trusted:true}` → frame `<p>`: `iframe: TRUSTED click`,
  `status: iframe button clicked (isTrusted = true)`. The banner appeared and cleared. Frame-offset
  `centerOf` is therefore correct **at 100% zoom / DPR 1**; HiDPI and non-100% zoom remain unverified
  (see Phase D / the "Trusted-click coordinates" limitation).
- **PERC-8 ★** — none of the four decoys appear in any snapshot. `Decoy: aria-hidden` and `Decoy: inert`
  are *visibly rendered* in the screenshot, so their exclusion is semantic pruning rather than absence.
  `Decoy: zero size` carries no `aria-hidden`/`inert`/`display:none`, so only the zero-size filter can
  drop it — confirming the `hasLayout(doc)` gate behaves correctly in real Chrome, which the jsdom unit
  tests cannot prove.
- **PERC-9** — snapshot lists `spinbutton "Quantity" [ref=e12]`, `slider "Volume" [ref=e13]`,
  `listbox "Tags" [ref=e14]`, `textbox "Notes editor" [ref=e15]` (contenteditable),
  `tab "Details tab" [ref=e16]`, `button "Disabled action" [ref=e17] [disabled]`.
  `browser_type e12 "7"` → `status: qty = 7`; `browser_click e16` → `status: role=tab clicked`.

Failures / notes:
- None. One documentation defect found and fixed in this commit: SMOKE-2 previously expected the
  `status:` text in the snapshot; Phase C snapshots list only interactive elements, so that line is
  screenshot-only.

### Run 4 — 2026-09-03 (Phase B robustness: commits `7e35ff9`, `d9a0ba0`, `458f291`)

```
Date: 2026-09-03  Chrome version: 152  Node: 20+  OS: Windows 11 Pro 26200
Display: 1920-px window, Chrome zoom left at 150% from Run 3 (DPR 1.5, innerWidth 1280) — irrelevant to these cases

STAT-1 [P]  TRUST-3 [P*]  TRUST-8 [P]  CONN-6 [P]  CONN-7 [P]  CONN-5 [P]
(* passed via the Chrome ≥ 152 branch — see below)
```

Scope: the 17th tool, the debugger-error UX, dead-Chrome detection, port-busy self-healing, and the
long-deferred idle-keepalive soak. **All six pass; no code changes needed after the three commits.**

Evidence per case:

- **STAT-1** — `browser_status` → `WebSocket host: listening on 127.0.0.1:9234` / `Extension: connected` /
  `Active tab: [1108099065] Agent Bridge E2E Playground — http://localhost:8080/e2e-playground.html`.
- **TRUST-3** — with DevTools docked on the tab (the viewport screenshot was visibly narrower), a
  `trusted:true` click on `#trusted-only` **succeeded**: `status: TRUSTED click received`. Chrome 152 lets
  an extension debugger attach alongside DevTools, so the "one debugger per tab" conflict this case was
  written for no longer happens. `describeDebuggerError`'s DevTools branch stays as a defensive path for
  older Chrome / other debugger extensions; it is unit-tested but could not be triggered live. The case
  text now accepts either outcome.
- **TRUST-8** — a ~600-character `browser_type {trusted:true}`; Cancel clicked on the banner mid-typing →
  tool error: "The debugging session was cancelled mid-action (the 'is debugging this browser' banner's
  Cancel was clicked, or DevTools took over the tab) — retry the action." SW console:
  `[bridge] debugger detached from tab 1108099065 reason: canceled_by_user`. Retry typed `retry ok` →
  `status: TRUSTED input = retry ok`.
- **CONN-6** — all `chrome.exe` ended. The *first* `browser_status` poll already said `Extension: not
  connected` — Chrome's exit closed the TCP socket cleanly, so the server saw `close` immediately and the
  heartbeat was never needed (it covers a *hung* peer, which only the unit test exercises). After relaunch,
  `browser_status` showed connected + the restored tab and `browser_snapshot` worked; the server was not
  restarted.
- **CONN-7** — orphan `node server/dist/index.js` started first, then `/mcp` reconnect. `browser_status`:
  `WebSocket host: NOT listening (port 9234)` + `Problem: WebSocket port 9234 is busy (listen EADDRINUSE:
  address already in use 127.0.0.1:9234): another chrome-agent-bridge instance is probably still running …
  retries the bind every 5s …`; `browser_snapshot` failed with that same text (not the generic "not
  connected"). Orphan Ctrl-C'd → the next `browser_status` (well under 15 s later) was listening +
  connected, and `browser_snapshot` worked. Nothing restarted.
- **CONN-5** — Chrome left untouched from 22:42:08. Checks at 22:45:38 (+3.5 min) and 22:51:43 (+6 min
  more, 9.5 min total idle): `browser_status` connected, `browser_snapshot` instant, no reconnect delay.
  The offscreen-document socket survives MV3 service-worker culling.

Failures / notes:
- None. One expectation was wrong rather than the code: TRUST-3 assumed Chrome still enforces one
  debugger per tab; Chrome 152 does not.


### Run 5 — 2026-09-07 (Step 4 `browser_evaluate`: commits `8a13b30`, `d2c7a8a`, `0757542`, `376e4dd`)

```
Date: 2026-09-07  Chrome version: 152.0.0.0 (UA-CH brand "Google Chrome" 152)  Node: 20+  OS: Windows 11 Pro 26200
Display: 1536-px innerWidth, DPR 1.25 (OS scaling 125%), Chrome zoom 100%
Fixtures: http://localhost:8080/e2e-playground.html and .../e2e-playground-csp.html

EVAL-1 [P] EVAL-2 [P] EVAL-3 [P] EVAL-4 [F -> P] EVAL-5 [P] EVAL-6 [P] EVAL-7 [P*]
EVAL-8 [P] EVAL-9 [P] EVAL-10 [P] EVAL-11 [P] EVAL-12 [P] EVAL-13 [P] EVAL-14 [P] EVAL-15 [P]
EVAL-16 [-] EVAL-17 [-] EVAL-18 [P] EVAL-19 [P*] EVAL-20 [F -> P] EVAL-21 [P] EVAL-22 [P]

20 of 22 run, 20 passed. EVAL-4 and EVAL-20 failed on the first pass, were fixed in `376e4dd`, and
passed on a re-run against the rebuilt extension (see "Re-run" below).
(* passed against a corrected expectation — see the EVAL-7 / EVAL-19 rows in §4.7)
(- EVAL-16 and EVAL-17 deliberately not run: both need a human at the keyboard, and TRUST-3 /
   TRUST-8 already exercise the same two Chrome behaviours — an extension debugger attaching
   alongside DevTools, and a mid-action Cancel on the banner — through the very same `withDebugger`
   that `browser_evaluate` uses. Accepted as coverage by the maintainer on 2026-09-07.)
```

**One real defect found and fixed** (`fix(extension): unwrap a promise-valued completion value`):
`Runtime.evaluate {awaitPromise: true}` unwraps exactly **one** promise level, and under
`replMode: true` that level is Chrome's own async wrapper around the script. So any expression whose
*completion value* is itself a promise came back as `Promise {}` — including the
`(async () => { … })()` form `wrapExpression` emits for a bare `return` (EVAL-4) and any
`p.then(…)` (EVAL-20). `evaluateInPage` now resolves such a result with a second
`Runtime.awaitPromise` on the same deadline, gated by the new pure `pendingPromiseId(raw)`
(4 unit tests). Both cases were re-run against the rebuilt extension and pass — see "Re-run" below.

Evidence per case:

- **EVAL-1 ★** — `document.title` → `Agent Bridge E2E Playground`. Banner appeared and cleared.
- **EVAL-2 ★** — `window.__playground` → pretty JSON with `"version": "1"`, `"items": [1,2,3]`,
  `"secret": "[Function: secret]"`, `"node": "<button id=\"counter\"> \"Click counter: 0\""`,
  `"self": "[Circular]"`, and `big` cut after element `99` with `"… 400 more"` as its last element,
  then `(output truncated — narrow the expression, e.g. pick fields or slice the array)`. Every
  serialiser branch behaved in real Chrome exactly as the jsdom unit tests predicted.
- **EVAL-3 ★** — `(await fetch('/e2e-playground.html')).status` → `200`.
- **EVAL-4 ★** — `const r = await fetch('/e2e-playground.html'); return r.status` → **`Promise {}`**,
  not `200`. Root-caused live: `await (async () => (await fetch('/e2e-playground.html')).status)()`
  — which the `\breturn\b` heuristic leaves unwrapped — returned `200`, and
  `await (async () => { … r.status })()` returned `undefined`, so `await` *was* being honoured and
  the missing piece was a second unwrap. Fixed as above; **passes on re-run**.
- **EVAL-5 ★** — `throw new Error("boom")` → tool error `[chrome.debugger] Error: boom` +
  `    at <anonymous>:1:7`. Immediate, not a 30 s hang.
- **EVAL-6** — `await Promise.reject(new TypeError("nope"))` → `TypeError: nope` + `at <anonymous>:1:22`.
- **EVAL-7** — `foo(` → `SyntaxError: Unexpected end of input`. Clear, but with neither the `Uncaught`
  prefix nor a line/col: under `replMode` Chrome hands back a real `exception` object, so
  `formatException` takes its `description` branch rather than the `text` + position branch. The
  §4.7 expectation was corrected rather than the code — both branches stay unit-tested.
- **EVAL-8 ★** — `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 20000))` with `timeoutMs: 1000` → in ~1 s,
  `Timed out after 1s — the debugger was detached; page-side work already started (e.g. a fetch)
  continues`. The next call (`document.title`) worked immediately; the banner was gone.
- **EVAL-9** — `while(true){}` with `timeoutMs: 1000` → the identical `Timed out after 1s …` message,
  and **the tab was fully responsive straight afterwards** (`document.title + document.readyState`
  returned instantly). *Which CDP branch:* the two branches are byte-identical to the agent by design
  (`timeoutMessage(ms)` is shared by `raceTimeout` and the `isExecutionTerminated` re-map), so the
  message cannot discriminate. What this run does pin: (a) Chrome 152 **does** honour
  `Runtime.evaluate.timeout` for synchronous code — V8 execution really was terminated, or the
  renderer main thread would still have been spinning and the next evaluate could not have run at
  all; and (b) the branch that *reports* is the bridge-side `raceTimeout`, because its deadline is
  set before `withDebugger` attaches (~100 ms) and so always expires before CDP's own timer, which
  starts only when evaluation begins. Both paths are implemented and render identically, so no code
  change was needed — this is the behaviour plan task E2.2 asked to pin.
- **EVAL-10** — `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 35000)); 1` with `timeoutMs: 40000` → `1` after
  ~35 s. The per-call timeout (`bridge.call(…, {timeoutMs: 45000})`) beats the old fixed 30 s
  connection timeout, which would have failed with "Timed out after 30000ms calling evaluate".
- **EVAL-11** — `const a = 1; a + 1` → `2`, and `2` again on an immediate repeat. `replMode` gives
  both the completion value and `const` re-declaration.
- **EVAL-12 ★** — on the CSP fixture, `new Function("return 1")()` → `1`. (The `\breturn\b` heuristic
  correctly left it unwrapped — the word is inside a string literal.) The page's own `#eval-probe`
  line reads `page eval: BLOCKED — EvalError: Evaluating a string as JavaScript violates the
  following Content Security Policy directive because 'unsafe-eval' is not an allowed source of
  script: script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`. Same call, same page: refused from page code, allowed
  over CDP. Design option A (`executeScript` + `new Function`) would have failed here.
- **EVAL-13 ★** — `document.querySelector('#counter').click(); document.querySelector('#count').textContent`
  → `1`; the counter and the `status:` line both moved.
- **EVAL-14** — `document.querySelectorAll('button')` → `NodeList [ "<button id=\"submit-btn\"> \"Sign
  in\"", … ]`, ten node descriptions — including the four decoys, because the serialiser walks the
  DOM rather than the a11y tree. Correct, and a useful contrast with `browser_snapshot`. No
  `(DOM node — …)` hint: kind is json.
- **EVAL-15** — `document.body` → `<body> "Agent Bridge E2E Playground status: counter = 1 Form Email
  address Password Favo…"` followed by `(DOM node — use browser_snapshot refs to act on it)`.
- **EVAL-16 / EVAL-17** — deliberately not run: both need a human at the keyboard (open DevTools on
  the tab; click the banner's Cancel mid-run). TRUST-3 and TRUST-8 already cover those two Chrome
  behaviours for the trusted-input path, and `browser_evaluate` reaches them through the same
  `withDebugger` — the attach and the detach-reason handling are shared code, not per-tool. The
  maintainer accepted that as coverage on 2026-09-07; re-open these two if `withDebugger` ever grows
  a per-caller attach path.
- **EVAL-18** — a `chrome://extensions` tab made active, then `document.title` → `browser_evaluate is
  not available here: the active tab is a restricted URL (chrome://, the New Tab page, or the Chrome
  Web Store) where extensions can't attach a debugger. (Cannot access a chrome:// URL)`. The E2.1
  `what`-threading works: the message names `browser_evaluate`, not "Trusted input".
- **EVAL-19** — `'x'.repeat(50000)` → 20 000 `x`s + `… [truncated: 30000 more chars]` + the
  truncation hint. The limit that applied is `maxChars`, not `maxString`; §4.7 corrected.
- **EVAL-20** — `navigator.clipboard.writeText("hi").then(() => "ok")` → **`Promise {}`** — the same
  defect as EVAL-4. `userGesture` itself stayed unproven on this pass; **passes on re-run**.
- **EVAL-21** — `JSON.stringify([typeof window.__agentBridge, typeof window.__playground])` →
  `["undefined","object"]`. The ISOLATED-world content script is invisible to page code and the
  page's own globals are visible, so `browser_evaluate` really is running in MAIN.
- **EVAL-22** — regression smoke after the `withDebugger(…, what)` signature change: `browser_click e6`
  → `count=1` (ACT-2); `browser_click {e7, trusted:true}` → `status: TRUSTED click received`
  (TRUST-2); `browser_screenshot {fullPage:true}` → a taller PNG containing `BOTTOM MARKER` (PERC-4).
  No regressions.

#### Re-run after `376e4dd` (rebuilt extension, reloaded) — 2026-09-07

```
EVAL-4 [P]  EVAL-20 [P]           (the two cases the fix targets)
EVAL-3 [P]  EVAL-14 [P]           (smoke: the unwrap must not disturb the paths that already worked)
```

- **EVAL-4 ★** — `const r = await fetch('/e2e-playground.html'); return r.status` → **`200`**. The
  `(async () => { … })()` wrapper's promise is now resolved by the follow-up `Runtime.awaitPromise`.
- **EVAL-20** — took two attempts, and the first one is the more interesting result:
  `navigator.clipboard.writeText("hi").then(() => "ok")` first returned a *tool error*,
  `NotAllowedError: Failed to execute 'writeText' on 'Clipboard': Document is not focused.` That is
  the fix working — the promise was resolved (well, rejected) and its rejection surfaced as an error
  instead of the old silent `Promise {}`. The cause was environmental: the Chrome **window** did not
  have OS focus while the agent drove it from a terminal, and `document.hasFocus()` is a separate
  requirement from transient activation that no `Runtime.evaluate` option can satisfy. A direct probe
  confirmed the split — `{"hasFocus":false,"userActivationActive":true,"userActivationSticky":true}`
  — i.e. `userGesture: true` **was** granting activation all along. After `browser_select_tab` gave
  the window focus, the verbatim expression returned **`ok`**.
- **EVAL-3 ★** — `(await fetch('/e2e-playground.html')).status` → `200`, unchanged.
- **EVAL-14** — `document.querySelectorAll('button')` → the same ten node descriptions as before: a
  non-promise object still routes to the in-page serialiser, untouched by the unwrap.
- Extra smoke, not a numbered case: `Promise.resolve({a: 1, b: [2, 3]})` → the pretty JSON for
  `{a, b}`, proving the unwrapped value then goes through the serialiser exactly like a direct one.

**Note for the docs (and for anyone writing EVAL-20 again):** `userGesture: true` supplies transient
activation, not window focus. Clipboard writes, and any other API that calls `document.hasFocus()`,
still fail with `NotAllowedError` when Chrome is in the background — which is the normal state while
an agent drives it. That is a Chrome rule, not a bridge limitation, and the error message says so
clearly enough to act on.

Failures / notes:
- One code defect (the promise-valued completion value) — fixed with a unit test in `376e4dd`, and
  both affected cases pass on the re-run below. No open failures.
- Two plan expectations were wrong rather than the code (EVAL-7, EVAL-19); §4.7 corrected to match
  what Chrome 152 actually does.
- EVAL-16 and EVAL-17 remain unrun (human interaction required).
- Environment note: port 9234 was initially held by orphaned `node server/dist/index.js` processes
  from earlier Claude sessions. `browser_status` diagnosed it exactly as CONN-7 describes, and the run
  started once they were closed — one session at a time really is a hard constraint.

```
status: 501 Unsupported method ('POST')   from cache: no   ip: ::1   size: 357 B
```
